# Provider watch — 2026-11-09

- Five services fail on their shipped defaults, for a user who does nothing unusual. `heygen/video` is the sharpest: the default `service_type` is `INTERACTIVE_AVATAR`, whose `/v1/streaming.*` endpoints now 404 — HeyGen's 2026-10-31 retirement has passed, so omitting `service_type` gets `Failed to setup HeyGenClient` and a pipeline with no avatar, while the class docstring still advertises the retired default (`reports/heygen/video/2026-11-09.md`). `azure/image` is the same shape and nine weeks old: `run_image_gen` still posts to `images/generations:submit` at api-version `2023-06-01-preview`, a DALL-E 2 flow whose api-version retired 2024-07-01, while every image model Azure documents today is served synchronously by `openai/v1/images/generations` and returns `b64_json` where the service reads `result.data[0].url`; no Azure image key is configured here, so it cannot even be probed. `xai/stt` silently discards every audio frame that arrives before `transcript.created` — in the probe all 354 of them, with no warning and no error. `openai/stt` treats `input_audio_buffer_commit_empty` as fatal, so a sub-100 ms commit tears down and reconnects the session (0/5 probe runs on both realtime models, against 4/5 truncated on 2026-10-28; an ad-hoc client pacing audio got 4/4 full transcripts, so the loss is the fatal handling, not the server). The `funasr` extra does not yield a runnable install at all — no torch, no fbank backend — so the probe loads the 936 MB checkpoint and then raises; overlaying kaldi-native-fbank makes the same env transcribe at TTFB 614 ms. Quieter but worse to debug: `aws/stt` logs "Converting from 24000 Hz to 16000 Hz" and declares 16000 in the presigned URL while forwarding the bytes untouched — nothing resamples, and AWS accepts 8000-48000 anyway. Dated: Sarvam retires `bulbul:v3` on Voice Agents tomorrow, 2026-11-10, with the Text-to-Speech API explicitly exempt, so `sarvam/tts` only inherits a retiring default name; `smallest/tts` carries two (`lightning_v3.1` retires 2026-11-30 and `sophia`, the `LIGHTNING_V3_1` default voice, exists only in the retiring catalogue; on 2026-11-28 the Pro checkpoint promoted for en/es/hi — the service's own default configuration — stops emitting word timestamps for `meher`); and `fireworks/llm`'s default has already passed its 2026-11-07 serverless retirement, still answering, with no drop-in successor.

- A second tier fails only on a configuration the provider's own pages hand you, and none of it is new. `deepgram/stt` has two routes to a permanently retired service: both classes hardcode `diarize=False`, so Deepgram's replacement `diarize_model` is rejected as a conflicting pair (400 at the handshake), and any `extra`/`addons` key the deepgram-sdk `connect()` signature does not declare raises TypeError, is retried three times and then retires the service — which is what puts `filler_words`, `measurements` and `redact_usage` out of reach. `gladia/stt` sends `language_config.languages` verbatim, so a regional `Language.EN_US` reaches Gladia as `en-US` and fails session init with a 400; our examples miss it only because `Language.EN` is already `en`. `speechmatics/stt` still has both six-week-old crashes (`punctuation_overrides` rejected as a `protocol_error`; `Settings(turn_detection_mode="vad")` raising `AttributeError` at StartFrame, which is the string form Speechmatics' own Pipecat page passes), and `speechmatics/tts` 422s when the caller leaves `sample_rate=None`. `openai/llm`'s `temperature` and `max_tokens` make every current OpenAI model answer 400, classified permanent. `fal/stt`'s documented `language=None` auto-detect swallows the utterance into an ErrorFrame; `xai/realtime` raises on every assistant audio delta when an `AudioOutput` omits `format`; `cartesia/tts` crashes on a dict-valued `generation_config` and `anthropic/llm` on a dict in `Settings.thinking`. Three shipped entry points hand out code that cannot work: `azure/realtime`'s example bot and both CLI registry entries never set `model`, so the URL carries OpenAI's `gpt-realtime-2.1` where Azure expects a deployment name; `google/vertex-realtime`'s `pipecat init` snippet is, nine weeks on, still the only one of 108 `SERVICE_CONFIGS` entries that fails `compile()`; and the Piper page on docs.pipecat.ai builds `PiperHttpTTSService` against a base URL that answers 405.

- The gpt-4.1 cluster has grown past the three LLM services into the eval harness, and Anthropic moved under us today. The 2027-01-14 shutdown covers `openai/llm`, `openai/responses-llm` and `openrouter/llm`, takes the `gpt-4o`/`gpt-4o-mini` aliases with it — including `DEFAULT_OPENAI_MODEL = "gpt-4o"` at `src/pipecat/evals/services.py:349`, the model that judges scenarios and plays personas when none is named — and pins in `evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml` and the five `examples/flows/yaml` bots retire alongside; `azure/llm` and `inworld/realtime` carry the same default one layer down. The successor OpenAI names regresses on both bump criteria (aiewf-eval 91.8% against gpt-4.1's 95.9%, slower in every probe) and at `effort="none"` — which `openai/responses-llm` ships as its reasoning default — completed 0 of 30 multi-turn conversations, so no unit can take this one alone. `openai/tts` is the same shape further on: every `/v1/audio/speech` model is deprecated with a 2027-01-06 shutdown and the named replacement is a Realtime model the speech endpoint refuses to serve. On Anthropic, Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 shipped today and enforce the preserved-thinking prefix check for every account, while Pipecat is not append-only — `LLMContextSummarizer._apply_summary` calls `set_messages`, and an injected "." user turn never enters the context — so replayed thinking blocks fail; the `anthropic` extra still pins `<2` against today's 2.7.0, whose API version removes `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k` for every model, widening a 400 that today only hits Opus 4.7 and later.

- Google needs two cross-unit decisions that no single unit can take. The Gemini default is split four ways — both Vertex classes ship `gemini-3.8-flash`, both Gemini API classes ship `gemini-3.6-flash`, and 3.8 is slower everywhere it was measured (`google/llm` 1616 ms against 1024 ms, `google/interactions-vertex-llm` 1072 ms against 699 ms, and on `google/vertex-llm` 2782 ms against 724 ms on a reasoning prompt, with one run in five producing no further chunk and tripping the 20 s `stream_idle_timeout_secs`, now for the third consecutive run). And `google-genai` 3.0.0 (2026-11-06) is outside the `<3` pin for nine units at once: `google/realtime` probed what raising it costs — the Gemini Developer API default moves to v1, where the Live connect 404s three times and then fails fatally and `google/llm`'s `-latest` aliases stop resolving, while Vertex pays only the httpx2 switch (`reports/google/realtime/2026-11-09.md`). A related edge lands on 2026-12-01, when `gemini-2.5-flash` and `gemini-2.5-pro` start being served by Gemini 3 models while `_THINKING_DEFAULTS`, keyed by the requested name, keeps sending them a `thinking_budget` the new family may ignore or reject.

- 36 PRs stand across the date, 22 of them raised by this cycle, and no branches were left for review. Two of the older ones are merged (#5531, #5580) and `smallest/stt`'s #5518 was closed unmerged on 2026-11-07 with both halves already in the code. One needs more than a review: `xai/tts` found that open PR #5562's description states the WebSocket endpoint has no `replace` equivalent, and a probe this run had the server accept `session.update` with the map and synthesize normally, so the two halves would conflict. 17 units could not probe and their findings are documentation-only, with whole providers dark: all three ElevenLabs units on a key the API rejects with 401, all three Sarvam units on one account out of credits (402), both Gradium units with no key for the fourth run running, both Zoom AI units, `azure/voice_live-realtime`, plus `bland/tts`, `camb/tts`, `meta/stt`, `sambanova/llm` and `crusoe/llm`, now unprobed for five runs on a 401. `azure/realtime` is an environment fix rather than a credential one: `AZURE_REALTIME_BASE_URL` names a host that does not resolve and still carries the retired preview path.

**116 units researched** — 36 PRs, 0 branches, 545 changes to consider, 17 errors, 0 with nothing new.

## Changes to consider

**High**

- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-09.md) — No reasoning-defaults table, so a Bedrock reasoning model runs at the provider's default (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — re-measured on a reasoning-triggering prompt: `us.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1` answers in TTFAT 3048 ms untouched against 1238 ms with `effort=low`, and `us.xai.grok-4.7` in 9007 ms against 5351 ms. Kimi K3.1 also reasons on "What is the capital of France?" untouched. The service has the `effort` setting to fix this with but keeps no `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` table, which CONTRIBUTING.md makes the rule for every LLM service. — *needs a call: what a Bedrock table keys on — model ids carry region and vendor prefixes — and what to do for models whose reasoning control is vendor-specific (Claude's `thinking` in `additionalModelRequestFields`) rather than Converse's `effort`*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-09.md) — `invoke_agent_runtime` is called without `runtimeSessionId`, so turns are not pinned to one AgentCore session (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — AWS's session guide is unchanged and explicit: generate a unique ID of at least 33 characters per user or conversation, pass the same ID for all related invocations, and "without a consistent session ID, each request may be routed to a new microVM, which may result in additional latency due to cold starts". The request header is 33-256 characters; the response returns the ID and the guide tells clients to capture it, so an echo-back scheme is possible. The processor sends neither and reads neither, and the default payload transformer sends only the latest user message, so the agent gets continuity from neither side. November's V2 snapshot-start feature shortens the cold start but does not restore session context. — *needs a call: Where the ID comes from — a constructor argument, one generated per conversation, or the ID echoed from the first response — and whether teardown should call StopRuntimeSession.*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Sample rates other than 8000/16000 Hz are relabelled 16000 Hz instead of resampled (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `_connect_websocket` logs "Converting from 24000 Hz to 16000 Hz" and declares 16000 in the presigned URL, but nothing converts — no resampler sits between the pipeline and `run_stt`, which forwards the bytes untouched. The clamp is unnecessary: presigned URLs declaring 24000 and 48000 were both accepted, and `MediaSampleRateHertz` is documented as 8000-48000. docs.pipecat.ai now says the audio is not resampled; the service docstring and the warning text still claim AWS supports only 8000/16000 Hz and that a conversion happens. — *needs a call: Which fix — pass the pipeline rate through when it is within 8000-48000 Hz, or resample to 16 kHz the way `OpenAIRealtimeSTTService` does with `create_stream_resampler`.*
- [azure/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/image/2026-11-09.md) — Service calls the retired `images/generations:submit` endpoint with api-version 2023-06-01-preview (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `run_image_gen` posts to `{endpoint}openai/images/generations:submit?api-version=2023-06-01-preview` and polls `operation-location`. That api-version retired 2024-07-01 and the submit/poll flow was DALL-E 2's; dall-e-2 retired 2025-01-27 and dall-e-3 on 2026-03-04 with existing deployments non-functional. Every image model Azure documents today (gpt-image-2.5-sunburst / -flare and gpt-image-2 GA; gpt-image-1/-mini/-1.5 limited-access preview) is served synchronously by `POST {endpoint}openai/v1/images/generations` (or the deployment-scoped `images/generations` at api-version 2025-04-01-preview); the how-to page states the call is synchronous and documents no `:submit` or operation-location flow. The request also never sends `model`, so `AzureImageGenSettings.model` is silently ignored, and the response is read as `result.data[0].url` while the whole gpt-image-* family returns `b64_json` only. — *needs a call: Porting to the v1 images endpoint changes the request, the response decoding (base64 to `URLImageRawFrame` or a plain image frame) and the polling loop at once - a behavioral rewrite that needs a maintainer's call on scope and a live Azure image deployment to verify; no AZURE_DALLE_API_KEY is configured here.*
- [azure/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default gpt-4.1 is Deprecated on Azure's schedule (new subscriptions cannot deploy it; retires 2027-04-14) (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Schedule unchanged since 2026-09-21 - gpt-4.1 (2025-04-14) Deprecated, retirement 2027-04-14, Replacement column still empty - so Azure still designates no successor for it, while naming gpt-5.1 for gpt-4o and gpt-5.6-sol/gpt-5.6-terra for the o-series. What changed is the quality evidence: aiewf-eval aiwf_medium_context was re-run and gpt-4.1 now sits at 95.9% / 743 ms TTFAT P50, with gpt-5.6-sol (none) 96.9% / 765 ms (+3.0%) and gpt-5.5 (none) 98.2% / 780 ms (+5.0%) both above it on pass rate and inside the 10% latency allowance; gpt-5.1 fell to 93.5% and is no longer a candidate, and the only gpt-6 row, gpt-6-luna (low) 91.8%, is below gpt-4.1. The blocker is now purely the probe: the test resource deploys gpt-4.1 and gpt-4o only, and a probe of gpt-5.6-sol returns 404 DeploymentNotFound, so no Azure-side latency comparison exists. On Azure the model string is a deployment name, so the default only matters for users who name their deployment after the model. — *needs a call: whether to adopt gpt-5.6-sol (or gpt-5.5) as the default on OpenAI-direct benchmark evidence alone, given Azure lists no replacement for gpt-4.1 and the test resource has no candidate deployment to measure latency against*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Example bot and CLI scaffold never set Settings.model, so the URL carries OpenAI's default gpt-realtime-2.1 instead of the Azure deployment (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `examples/realtime/realtime-azure.py:101` and the `azure_realtime` entries in `src/pipecat/cli/registry/_configs.py:663` / `service_metadata.py:1277` build the service with no `model`, so the base class appends `?model=gpt-realtime-2.1` (`openai/realtime/llm.py:341`). On Azure that query parameter is the deployment name, not a model name, so a fresh bot only connects if the user happened to name their deployment `gpt-realtime-2.1`. — *needs a call: which env var should name the deployment (the scaffold's env template emits only `AZURE_REALTIME_API_KEY` and `AZURE_REALTIME_BASE_URL`), and whether the service should refuse to connect when no deployment is named*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Default gpt-4o-mini is on Foundry's Deprecated lifecycle (new customers blocked), retiring 2027-04-14 (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — Voice Live still lists gpt-4o-mini in its supported-models table (Standard tier), but the Foundry retirement schedule marks gpt-4o-mini 2024-07-18 Deprecated with retirement 2027-04-14 and no declared replacement. Standard-tier successors in Voice Live are gpt-5.6-luna (GA, retires 2028-01-11), gpt-5-mini (GA, now 2027-07-13) and the native-audio gpt-realtime-2.1-mini (GA, 2027-06-25). The newly listed gpt-6.1-sol (Pro) and gpt-6-luna (Lite) have no lifecycle entry yet. — *needs a call: Microsoft names no replacement, and the natural successors split two ways: a cascaded text model (gpt-5.6-luna / gpt-5-mini, same Azure-TTS voice behaviour) or a native-audio one (gpt-realtime-2.1-mini, which changes voice handling). Which line the default should follow is a maintainer's call, and no credential was available to probe either.*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Deepgram deprecated `diarize` in favour of `diarize_model`, and Pipecat sends `diarize` unconditionally, so `diarize_model` is rejected (since 2026-08-25, 10 weeks) — Both classes hardcode `diarize=False` in their default settings, so every connection carries `diarize=false`. Deepgram rejects a request that sets both parameters: `extra={"diarize_model": "latest"}` fails the handshake with 400 and permanently retires the service (re-probed on deepgram-sdk 7.17.0). `diarize_model=latest` alone connects. The undocumented workaround is `Settings(diarize=None, extra={"diarize_model": "latest"})`, which is outside the field's declared `bool | NotGiven` type. The `/v1/listen` reference still carries "Deprecated. Use `diarize_model` instead." on `diarize`, with no removal date. On self-hosted releases from `release-260514`, `diarize=true` returns no speaker labels at all because only the v2 diarizer is installed. — *needs a call: whether to drop `diarize` from the default request and add a `diarize_model` field, or map `diarize=True` onto `diarize_model="v1"` and deprecate the field outright*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `extra`/`addons` keys the deepgram-sdk `connect()` signature does not declare kill the connection, so `filler_words`, `measurements` and `redact_usage` are unreachable (since 2026-08-25, 10 weeks) — `_build_connect_kwargs` spreads `extra`/`addons` into `client.listen.v1.connect(**kwargs)`, which takes only the AsyncAPI query params; an undeclared key raises TypeError inside `_connection_handler`, is retried 3x and then retires the service. On deepgram-sdk 7.17.0 the signature has grown `diarize_model` and `language_hint` but still omits `filler_words`, `measurements`, `keyterms` and `redact_usage`, all of which the `/v1/listen` reference documents. The SDK spreads `request_options["additional_query_parameters"]` into the WebSocket query string, so routing undeclared keys there would restore the hatch. `DeepgramSageMakerSTTService` builds its own query string and already accepts these keys. — *needs a call: a call on whether undeclared keys should be forwarded to Deepgram unvalidated or rejected at construction time instead of failing mid-run*
- [fal/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fal/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Language auto-detect (`settings=Settings(language=None)`) produces no transcript (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Wizper documents `language: null` as auto-detect; the request succeeds but `Language(self._settings.language)` in `run_stt` raises `None is not a valid Language`, so the utterance is swallowed into an ErrorFrame. Re-probed 2026-11-09, still fails. — *needs a call: a call on what `TranscriptionFrame.language` should carry when fal auto-detects — the response has a `languages` list, not a single code*
- [fireworks/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fireworks/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default `nemotron-3-ultra-nvfp4` is on Fireworks' Serverless retirement list (announced 2026-10-19, effective 2026-11-07) — the retirement date has passed but the model still answers (5/5 probes, median TTFAT 257 ms), so nothing is broken yet. Fireworks' named replacement is a dedicated deployment of Nemotron Super VL, which has no serverless model ID, so there is no drop-in successor. Probed serverless stand-ins, all working - `glm-5p3` (median TTFAT 706 ms), `deepseek-v4p1-flash-1017` (653 ms), `muse-spark-open` (5323 ms, 4.7 s of it reasoning by default) — *needs a call: which serverless model becomes the default - Fireworks designates none, `deepseek-v4p1-flash-1017` has no aiewf-eval row, and glm-5.3's 97.7% row ran at `low` reasoning rather than the Fireworks defaults this service sends*
- [funasr/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/funasr/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The `funasr` extra does not yield a runnable install (no torch, no fbank backend) (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — funasr 1.4.24 (latest, 2026-11-08) still declares neither torch, torchaudio nor kaldi-native-fbank; only the `knf` extra supplies kaldi-native-fbank. The repo's locked env now holds funasr 1.4.23 + torch 2.15 (torch arrives via `local-smart-turn`, not via this extra) and no fbank backend, so `probe.py run` loads the 936 MB checkpoint and then raises on the segment from `funasr.utils.fbank`. Overlaying kaldi-native-fbank 1.23.0 on that same env makes the probe transcribe correctly at TTFB 614 ms. `funasr = ["funasr[knf]>=1.4.16"]` plus a torch decision and `uv lock` covers it; the floor also matters because the unbounded `>=1.1.0` pin still allows 1.4.2 (import fails without torchaudio) and 1.4.14 (caps `numpy<2`). — *needs a call: which fbank backend the extra should require (kaldi-native-fbank via `funasr[knf]`, or torchaudio pinned to torch as `local-smart-turn` does), and whether the extra may pull torch itself*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `language_config.languages` is sent verbatim, so a regional `Language` (e.g. `Language.EN_US`) fails session init with a 400 — `_prepare_settings` calls `language_config.model_dump()` without routing entries through `language_to_gladia_language`, so `Language.EN_US` reaches Gladia as `en-US`. Ad-hoc init POST this run: `languages: ["en-US"]` → 400 listing the iso639-1 values it accepts; `["en"]` → 201. `_setup_gladia` raises on a non-ok response, so the service cannot start. Our examples happen to pass `Language.EN`, which is already `en`. — *needs a call: whether `language_config.languages` should be converted like other language inputs (and typed to accept `Language`), or validated with a clear error instead*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-09.md) — Live sessions end after 10 minutes; the service treats the close as a failure and has no session resumption (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — docs still cap live transcription at 10 minutes per session; `_receive_handler` catches the close, calls `push_error`, and reopens, so every long call sees an ErrorFrame plus a gap in transcripts. `go_away` and `session_resumption_update` are ignored, though `GeminiLiveLLMService` already handles both. — *needs a call: a call on whether STT should reuse the LLM service's session-resumption handling, or just stop reporting an expected expiry as an error*
- [google/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/image/2026-11-09.md) — Default gemini-3.1-flash-image was deprecated on 2026-10-06 in favour of gemini-nano-banana-2.1 (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — re-probed today, the gap widened: 11.3 s vs 8.4 s median wall clock over 3 interleaved rounds (~35%, past the 10% bar for an automatic bump); both work unchanged through the class and no shutdown date is announced, so nothing breaks today — *needs a call: whether a ~35% slower generation is an acceptable price for moving off a deprecated default, or whether the faster gemini-3.1-flash-lite-image is the better target*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-09.md) — gemini-2.5-flash and gemini-2.5-pro route to Gemini 3 models on 2026-12-01, but their _THINKING_DEFAULTS entries still send a thinking_budget — Google deprecated both on 2026-11-02 — from 2026-12-01 gemini-2.5-flash is served by gemini-3.5-flash-lite and gemini-2.5-pro by gemini-3.8-flash. The table is keyed by the requested name, so those requests keep carrying thinking_budget 0 / 128 to a model family that takes thinking_level, which the service's own warning says a Gemini 3 model may ignore or reject. — *needs a call: drop the two entries on 2026-12-01, or map the retired names onto the successor's thinking_level so a user who never changes the model keeps thinking off?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-09.md) — The gemini-3.8-flash default is 2-4x slower on Vertex than gemini-3.6-flash and intermittently fails to complete (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — Third consecutive run showing it. Interleaved medians of 5 on a trivial prompt: 3.8 Flash TTFAT 1365 ms (5/5, spread 971-2597 ms) against 611 ms (5/5, 542-778 ms) for 3.6 Flash. On a multi-turn-style reasoning prompt: 3.8 Flash 2782 ms (4/5, spread 1004-5963 ms) against 724 ms (5/5, 686-811 ms); the fifth 3.8 Flash run produced no further chunk and tripped the 20 s stream_idle_timeout_secs set in GoogleLLMService.__init__, pushing `LLM completion timeout`. thinking_ms is 0 throughout, so this is not reasoning time. The quality case is unchanged and still favours the new default - aiewf-eval medium-context has gemini-3.8-flash (low) at 99.9% / 1411 ms P50 against gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) at 95.1% / 808 ms - but the retirement cited when commit 714573d00 made the bump still does not exist: the Gemini deprecations page lists gemini-3.6-flash as `No shutdown date announced` and the Agent Platform release notes through 2026-11-06 announce no Gemini text-model retirement. gemini-3.7-flash, whose leaderboard row matches 3.8 at 821 ms P50, is no faster on Vertex (1270 ms, median of 3), so it is not an escape hatch. GoogleLLMService (google/llm) still defaults to gemini-3.6-flash, so the two units disagree. — *needs a call: keep gemini-3.8-flash for its benchmark score and accept first tokens that are several times slower and occasionally never arrive, or go back to gemini-3.6-flash until Google announces a shutdown date - and should google/llm move in step either way?*
- [google/vertex-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `pipecat init` scaffold for Gemini Vertex Live emits a snippet with a stray `),` that fails to parse, and passes a credentials *path* to `credentials=` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — unchanged at 2c57b1978 and still the only one of the now 108 entries in `pipecat.cli.registry._configs.SERVICE_CONFIGS` that fails `compile()` (duplicated in `service_metadata.MANUAL_SERVICE_CONFIGS`); it also wires `credentials=os.getenv("GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS")` while the generated `.env` documents that variable as a file path, so the fixed snippet would still raise from `json.loads` — *needs a call: whether to fix only the parens plus `credentials_path=`, or also align the CLI's `GOOGLE_PROJECT_ID`/`GOOGLE_LOCATION`/`GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` names with the `GOOGLE_CLOUD_*` names the examples and docs use and the registry's `GOOGLE_VERTEX` prefix*
- [groq/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Service buffers the whole /audio/speech response instead of consuming Groq's chunked stream (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — re-measured today: the streaming client sees `transfer-encoding: chunked` with 86 chunks and first bytes at 0.231 s, while `run_tts` calls `create()` and the probe pipeline gets exactly one `TTSAudioRawFrame` at TTFB 1.048 s. `with_streaming_response` plus the base class's `_stream_audio_frames_from_iterator(strip_wav_header=True)` would stream and drop the per-chunk `wave.open` decode — *needs a call: rewriting `run_tts` is a behavioral change beyond a value swap, so it needs a maintainer to own the switch to the streaming client and the base-class audio helper*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-09.md) — Default service_type is INTERACTIVE_AVATAR, whose `/v1/streaming.*` endpoints are retired and return 404 (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — re-probed today: `POST /v1/streaming.new` and `/v1/streaming.create_token` return 404 while `/v2/avatars` answers 200 with the same key. HeyGen's v1/v2 retirement date of 2026-10-31 has now passed, so there is no path on which this backend comes back. A user who omits `service_type` gets `Failed to setup HeyGenClient` and a pipeline with no avatar; the class docstring still advertises the retired `Shawn_Therapist_public` / `v2` default. — *needs a call: whether to flip the default to LIVE_AVATAR now, or deprecate the Interactive Avatar path (api_interactive_avatar.py, ServiceType.INTERACTIVE_AVATAR) on the usual cycle*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Default `openai/gpt-4.1-mini` is shut down by OpenAI on 2027-01-14; the designated successor `openai/gpt-6-luna` is 63% slower to first token through Inworld — OpenAI's deprecation page schedules `gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` for 2027-01-14 with `gpt-6-luna` as the replacement, and Inworld's router drops a model once upstream retires it (`gpt-4.1-nano` is gone from the catalogue since its 2026-10-23 shutdown). Measured through the realtime API, median TTFAT is 504 ms for `openai/gpt-4.1-mini`, 821 ms for `openai/gpt-6-luna`, 602 ms for `openai/gpt-6-luna:fast` (3 turns each). aiewf-eval favours the successor on quality — `gpt-6-luna (low)` 91.8% vs `gpt-4.1-mini` 81.8% — but only at `low` reasoning effort, which this service cannot select. `examples/realtime/realtime-inworld-locally-driven-turns.py` pins the same string — *needs a call: whether to accept ~100-300 ms more first-token latency for the quality gain, pick `openai/gpt-6-luna:fast` (closest on latency but absent from aiewf-eval), or move off the OpenAI family entirely before 2027-01-14*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — A text-only session pushes no text — `response.output_text.delta` has no event model, so `output_modalities=["text"]` produces silence — with `output_modalities=["text"]` the server sends `response.output_text.delta` / `.done` and no `response.output_audio_transcript.delta`; `_server_event_types` in `events.py` registers neither, so `parse_server_event` returns `None` and the receive loop logs "ignoring unhandled server event". `_push_output_transcript_text_frames` is only ever reached from the audio-transcript branch, so no `LLMTextFrame` or `TTSTextFrame` is pushed at all. `output_modalities` is a user-settable `SessionProperties` field that `_create_response` forwards to `response.create` — *needs a call: whether a text-only session should push `TTSTextFrame` as well as `LLMTextFrame` (the audio path pushes both, but output aggregation in a session with no audio may not want them)*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Default voxtral-mini-transcribe-realtime-2602 was deprecated 2026-10-31 and retires 2027-04-30 — Mistral names voxtral-mini-transcribe-realtime-3 as the alternative; it works unchanged through MistralSTTService, but it is Public Preview and measured TTFB 2955 ms vs 1310 ms (medians of 5), and target_streaming_delay_ms=240 did not close the gap. A later bump also needs MISTRAL_TTFS_P99 (1.89 s) re-measured. — *needs a call: whether to wait for realtime 3 to reach GA and get faster, or bump early and accept the latency, given the 2027-04-30 retirement*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Default voxtral-mini-tts-2603 is deprecated (2026-10-31, retires 2027-04-30); Mistral names voxtral-mini-tts-3-0 as its alternative — Voxtral TTS 3 works through MistralTTSService unchanged, but on the saved cloned voice both models accept its median TTFB is 725 ms vs 576 ms (round 1) and 859 ms vs 752 ms (round 2), outside the 10% tolerance; on a TTS 3 preset voice (`gb_jane_neutral`, which 2603 rejects) it is 580 ms, so the gap tracks the cloned-voice path rather than the model. The Voxtral 3 expression adapter already in the tree only engages for `voxtral-mini-tts-3*` models, so the shipped default performs no expression tags. — *needs a call: whether to bump despite the slower cloned-voice TTFB, or wait for the cloned-voice path to catch up while 26.03 is still served*
- [novita/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/novita/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default moonshotai/kimi-k2.5 is retired upstream (Moonshot 2026-08-31) and still served only by hosts like Novita (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Moonshot now lists kimi-k3.1-pro as flagship and demotes kimi-k3 to previous-generation; kimi-k2.5 is flat "Deprecated" with no date and Novita still serves it with no sunset notice. Our probe gives kimi-k2.5 TTFAT 1266 ms (median of 5) against 2997 ms for kimi-k3.1-pro and 2932 ms for kimi-k3.1-pro-highspeed with thinking on by default, 1453 ms / 1954 ms with thinking off. aiewf-eval has no row for kimi-k2.5, kimi-k3 or kimi-k3.1-pro, so the required pass-rate comparison still cannot be made — *needs a call: a call on which successor to adopt while aiewf-eval has benchmarked none of them, and whether shipping it with thinking disabled — a behavior change, not a value change — is acceptable*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default gpt-4.1 is scheduled for shutdown on 2027-01-14 (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — unchanged since 2026-10-28 — the deprecations table still reads GPT-4.1 (gpt-4.1-2025-04-14) → gpt-6-luna, announced 2026-10-14. gpt-6-luna is still below the default on aiewf-eval medium-context (91.8% / 917 ms at low effort vs gpt-4.1's 95.9% / 743 ms, `none` unranked at 0 of 30) and still slower here (TTFAT 856 ms vs 474 ms, median of 5). gpt-6.1-terra still has no leaderboard row. The retirement also takes the gpt-4o and gpt-4o-mini aliases, and gpt-4.1 is pinned in evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml and the flows examples, so the decision moves more than the service default. — *needs a call: which model replaces gpt-4.1 before 2027-01-14 — OpenAI's named gpt-6-luna at a lower pass rate, the unbenchmarked gpt-6.1-terra, or gpt-5.6-terra from the generation OpenAI is retiring tier by tier*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `temperature` and `max_tokens` Settings make every current OpenAI model reject the request with a 400 (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — re-probed on gpt-6.1-terra: temperature=0.7 returns `unsupported_value` (only 1 is supported) and max_tokens=200 returns `unsupported_parameter` (use max_completion_tokens). Both are still classified permanent, so the worker logs "can no longer do its job" and the service is unusable for the session; the 2026-11-05 content-filter fix narrowed that path to content_filter 400s only. — *needs a call: a stance on per-model parameter handling — drop temperature and map max_tokens to max_completion_tokens for reasoning models, or fail with a clearer message*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — A session configured with an MCP tool hangs — conversation.item.added carries an item type ConversationItem rejects (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `SessionProperties.tools` accepts provider-native dicts, so OpenAI's `{"type": "mcp", ...}` reaches the server, the server answers with a `conversation.item.added` whose item is `mcp_list_tools`, and `ConversationItem.type` is still `Literal["message", "function_call", "function_call_output"]`. GA's `ConversationItem` union has nine members — the three message shapes, `function_call`, `function_call_output`, `mcp_list_tools`, `mcp_call`, `mcp_approval_request`, `mcp_approval_response`. PR 6329 (merged 2026-10-23) fixed the event half: unmodeled event *types* are skipped, so the eight `mcp_list_tools.*` / `response.mcp_call*` events no longer stop the receive loop. A modeled event failing validation still raises out of `_receive_task_handler`, which has no try/except, so the session goes quiet with no ErrorFrame — *needs a call: whether to model the MCP item types (and surface approval requests) or to make the receive loop log and skip any event that fails validation, not just unmodeled types*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default gpt-4.1 is deprecated and leaves the API on 2027-01-14 (since 2026-08-25, 10 weeks) — OpenAI's 2026-10-14 deprecation names gpt-6-luna as the replacement, but gpt-6-luna does not meet the quality gate — aiewf-eval medium-context ranks it 91.8% against gpt-4.1's 95.9%, and the effort="none" configuration the service sends by default completed 0 of 30 conversations. The leaderboard is unchanged this run: among benchmarked models only gpt-5.6-sol at effort="none" clears gpt-4.1 (96.9% vs 95.9%, TTFAT P50 765 ms vs 743 ms), and OpenAI names it as the successor to gpt-5 and o3, not to gpt-4.1; gpt-6.1-terra and gpt-6.1-sol are still unbenchmarked. The five examples under examples/flows/yaml pin gpt-4.1 explicitly and retire with it — *needs a call: which model replaces gpt-4.1 before 2027-01-14 — OpenAI's choice (gpt-6-luna) or the one that benchmarks at parity (gpt-5.6-sol); the same gpt-4.1 default is shared with openai/llm*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — OpenAIRealtimeSTTService treats input_audio_buffer_commit_empty as fatal, so a commit with under 100 ms of audio tears down and reconnects the session (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — two triggers, one cause. (a) A 60 ms speech segment appended and committed on a fully configured session (session.updated received, turn_detection null, no VAD events) comes back as input_audio_buffer_commit_empty - the server wants at least 100 ms - and _handle_error re-raises, so the receive loop drops the socket and reconnects. Any short VAD segment does this. (b) Audio appended inside the handshake window still gets the session's default turn detection, a server auto-commit, and then the same empty-buffer rejection on our commit. The window is narrow: session.updated lands 75-94 ms after the socket opens (3 sessions), so last run's reading that any user speaking early in a session loses audio overstates it - a real bot paces audio and connects at setup(). probe.py bursts the whole clip, which lands inside that window every time: 0/5 on both realtime models this run, with no transcript at all, against 4/5 with truncated text on 2026-10-28. Through an ad-hoc client appending from socket open at near-realtime pace, 4/4 sessions returned the full transcript (2/4 showed speech_started before session.updated), so the loss comes from the service's fatal handling, not from the server — *needs a call: whether an empty-buffer commit should be an error frame and a reconnect at all, rather than a logged no-op, and whether run_stt should also hold audio until _session_ready*
- [openai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Every /v1/audio/speech model is deprecated with a 2027-01-06 shutdown and the named replacement is a Realtime model (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — the deprecation table now lists all five rows separately (`tts-1`, `tts-1-hd`, `gpt-4o-mini-tts`, `gpt-4o-mini-tts-2025-03-20`, `gpt-4o-mini-tts-2025-12-15`), so our default alias is named in its own right; the replacement for every row is `gpt-realtime-2.1-mini`, which the TTS guide says is not supported on /v1/audio/speech. The alias still serves (TTFB 1372 ms median of 3) and the endpoint itself carries no deprecation — *needs a call: whether OpenAITTSService gets deprecated in favour of the Realtime service, or we keep it serving until 2027-01-06 and wait for OpenAI to offer a speech-API successor*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default openai/gpt-4.1 is scheduled for OpenAI shutdown on 2027-01-14 (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — OpenAI's deprecations page still carries the 2026-10-14 entry removing gpt-4.1 from the API on 2027-01-14 with gpt-6-luna as the replacement. OpenRouter serves the slug from Azure as well as OpenAI (three endpoints, all at 100% uptime, no sunset notice), so the slug itself is unlikely to go dark on that date. gpt-6-luna remains a regression on both bump criteria -- aiewf-eval 91.8% against gpt-4.1's 95.9%, and 926 ms against 738 ms median TTFAT in this run's probe. Models scoring above gpt-4.1 (gpt-5.6-terra medium 99.9%, gpt-5.5 none 98.2%, gpt-5.4 low 97.7%, gpt-5.6-sol none 96.9%) are not positioned as gpt-4.1's successor — *needs a call: which successor to take, given the designated one regresses on aiewf-eval and probes slower, and whether this wrapper should track OpenAILLMService's default rather than move on its own*
- [piper/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/piper/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The Piper page on docs.pipecat.ai builds PiperHttpTTSService with base_url="http://localhost:5000", which returns 405 (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — still the snippet on the page today; probed on 2026-09-01 against piper-tts 1.7.0's http_server: POST / -> 405, POST /synthesize -> 200. The page lives in the docs repo, not here — *needs a call: fix the snippet in the docs repo, or decide whether the service should append /synthesize itself — that would break setups whose base_url already names the endpoint or points at a legacy Piper server that serves POST /*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-09.md) — evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml calls qwen-turbo, which Alibaba decommissioned on 2026-10-10 (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — the row no longer runs — three isolated probes of qwen-turbo returned HTTP 429 "exceeded your current request limit" while qwen-plus and qwen-flash succeeded on the same key minutes apart, which is what Alibaba's decommission policy describes (QPM/TPM for a retired model is wound down before the id stops resolving). On 2026-10-12 the same probe still returned a normal completion at 979 ms. Alibaba names no single successor, only "the latest Qwen3.7/3.6 series models"; the surviving alias at the same tier is qwen-flash (probe ok, TTFAT 647 ms) — *needs a call: whether the turn-completion row should be dropped or repointed, and to which model — swapping a benchmark row changes what the corpus measures*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Resemble no longer sells voice generation; the TTS docs are an archive and new customers are not accepted — the CEO's 2026-08-13 post says "We're supporting our existing customers but not taking new ones" and the voice models "are open and free to use, but we won't be producing new ones for commercial sale"; docs.resemble.ai's index now lists only the Detect products and every voice page sits under /voice-archive, "retained for historical reference", which "do not imply continued availability of commercial voice services"; Voice Design was removed 2026-09-28; the hosted WebSocket still synthesizes for an existing key (probe below) and no shutdown date is published — *needs a call: a stance on a service whose provider has left the market — deprecate it, note the closed signup in our docs and the two examples, or leave it running for existing customers*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `_SUPPORTED_MODELS` admits `deepseekv4.1-flash`, a name /v2 no longer serves — The model was renamed `deepseekv4.1-flash-1017` when it went GA on 2026-10-30. The short name is gone from `GET /v2/models` and from the /v2 chat reference's model enum, which states that an unknown or wrongly cased ID returns 404. `_SUPPORTED_MODELS`, `_REASONING_MODELS` and `tests/test_sarvam_llm.py` all still carry it; PR #6138 added it on 2026-10-13, two weeks before the rename. — *needs a call: the Sarvam account returns 402 on every completion, so the 404 is documented but unconfirmed by request — a swap to `deepseekv4.1-flash-1017` needs one passing probe*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Reasoning cannot be turned off on the default sarvam-105b through Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — The service ships no reasoning-defaults table, so every model runs at Sarvam's own default — `max` on `glm5.3` and `kimi-k3.1-pro`, `high` on `glm5.3-flash` and the DeepSeek models, on by default on `qwen38-27b`. The /v2 reference now documents the lowest setting per model, which CONTRIBUTING's "Reasoning Defaults for LLM Services" asks the service to send: `none` for `deepseekv4.1-flash-1017`, `deepseekv4-pro` and `qwen38-27b`, `low` elsewhere. For `sarvam-105b` the model page gives the default as `low` and `reasoning_effort=None` as the off-switch, but `None` means do-not-send in our code, so the explicit null cannot be expressed. The `extra_body.chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false` escape hatch measured 885 ms TTFAT against 4053 ms on 2026-10-12. — *needs a call: whether to give Sarvam a `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` table keyed by model (the base `LLMService._apply_thinking_default` already exists) and how to express the documented explicit null that `reasoning_effort=None` currently swallows*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-09.md) — LANGUAGE_MAP does not match Pulse's streaming language set (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — 15 mapped codes (bg, cs, da, et, fi, hu, lt, lv, mt, pa, pl, ro, sk, sv, uk) are pre-recorded-only or undocumented on streaming; `language=pl` fails the WebSocket handshake and surfaces as an opaque reconnection error. The four streaming East Asian codes (zh, yue, ja, ko), `hi-dev`, and the three aggregators (north_indic, multi-asian, multi-south-indic) are absent, so they only reach the API through resolve_language's unverified fallback. The aggregators are also the only way to get Pulse's streaming language detection. East Asian codes and multi-asian are served only from `wss://api.us.smallest.ai`; hi-dev, ta, te, kn, ml and multi-south-indic are India-region only and return LANGUAGE_NOT_ENABLED_IN_REGION elsewhere. — *needs a call: trimming the map is a breaking change for anyone passing those codes today; decide between removing them, raising a clear error, or documenting the streaming/batch and region split*
- [smallest/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/tts/2026-11-09.md) — lightning_v3.1 retires 2026-11-30 while SmallestTTSModel.LIGHTNING_V3_1 and its sophia default voice remain — Smallest retires `lightning_v3.1` on 2026-11-30 18:30 UTC; after that the model value is served by `lightning_v3.1_pro`, whose voice catalogue is disjoint. `sophia`, the enum member's default voice, is in the v3.1 catalogue only (confirmed against the public get_voices endpoints), as are four of the five other voices the `word_timestamps` docstring names (`devansh`, `kartik`, `maithili`, `liam`, `avery`); only `meher` is a Pro voice. The shipped default model is unaffected. — *needs a call: whether to deprecate the enum member now (sarvam's `warn_deprecated` pattern needs a since-version) or drop it after retirement, and what the docstring should say about word-timestamp voices once only `meher` survives*
- [smallest/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/tts/2026-11-09.md) — the Pro checkpoint promoted on 2026-11-28 emits no word timestamps for meher — `lightning_v3.1_pro_10_26` becomes the stable `lightning_v3.1_pro` for en/es/hi (and for requests omitting language) on 2026-11-28 — exactly this service's default configuration. Two ad-hoc socket calls with `meher` returned zero `word_timestamp` frames on the preview checkpoint against ten on the current one, which contradicts the Pro model card. Nothing breaks: `_apply_force_complete` already covers providers that drop word events, so the turn's text arrives as one TTSTextFrame instead of per-word frames with PTS. — *needs a call: whether to raise the contradiction with Smallest before 2026-11-28 or document the loss of word-aligned text for English, Spanish and Hindi*
- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Setting `punctuation_overrides` makes the Agent STT session fail permanently (since 2026-09-28, 6 weeks) — `_build_config` still passes `punctuation_overrides` on the Agent STT `TranscriptionConfig` and the endpoint still answers `transcription_config: Additional property punctuation_overrides is not allowed`, re-probed this run. The service classes `protocol_error` as permanent, so the processor is marked unusable and no transcript ever arrives. The field is absent from the Agent STT WebSocket schema and from Speechmatics' own Pipecat page, which documents every other transcription setting we expose; only docs.pipecat.ai still presents it as a working setting. Unset by default, so only users who set it are hit. — *needs a call: dropping the field contradicts the deliberate vendor commit that kept it on Agent STT, so someone has to decide between deprecating it and waiting for the proxy to accept it*
- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-09.md) — A plain string for `turn_detection_mode` crashes the service at StartFrame (since 2026-09-28, 6 weeks) — `_handle_turn_detection_mode` does `mode.value`, so `Settings(turn_detection_mode="vad")` raises `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'value'` while processing StartFrame and the session never opens; re-probed this run, unchanged. `TurnDetectionMode` is a `StrEnum` and `Settings` is a plain dataclass that coerces nothing, so the string form is easy to reach — the vendor's own Pipecat page passes `turn_detection_mode` through `Settings`. `AgentTurnDetectionMode(mode)` accepts both the member and the string. — *needs a call: whether enum-typed Settings fields should coerce strings across the codebase, or whether the annotation is the contract and the right fix is a clear error instead*
- [together/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/llm/2026-11-09.md) — The shipped default reasons before every answer, costing seconds before the first spoken token (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — out of the box `zai-org/GLM-5.2` spent 10227 ms thinking on a scheduling question (TTFAT 10455 ms, median of 5) and 2859 ms on a trivial one (TTFAT 3907 ms, median of 5); with `reasoning={"enabled": false}` the same scheduling prompt answered in 268 ms (median of 5); `LLMService._apply_thinking_default` is the framework hook the Anthropic, Google, OpenAI Responses and Perplexity services use for exactly this, and `DeepSeekLLMService` ships `thinking` disabled outright; both Settings fields a Together table would need (`reasoning`, `reasoning_effort`) are now merged — *needs a call: whether TogetherLLMService should send GLM its reasoning-off default, as DeepSeekLLMService does for its V4 models and as #5446 did for Sonnet 5 and later*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — A user-supplied `AudioOutput` without a `format` is never backfilled, so `_get_output_sample_rate()` raises on every assistant audio delta (since 2026-09-28, 6 weeks) — `_ensure_audio_config` replaces a wholly missing `audio.input` / `audio.output` but never fills a missing `format` inside one, so `AudioOutput(speed=1.2)` — the documented way to slow the bot down — leaves `format=None` and the service raises RuntimeError at llm.py:394 for every `response.output_audio.delta`; the same shape on input makes `_get_configured_sample_rate("input")` return None, which also skips the audio-config rescue in `_update_settings` — *needs a call: a call on whether the missing `format` should be backfilled from the transport's sample rate (the fix changes what a today-broken config does) or the service should reject the incomplete config up front*
- [xai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Audio pushed before xAI's `transcript.created` is discarded, not buffered — `run_stt` drops every frame while `_session_ready` is clear (~250ms after connect, and again after each automatic reconnect). probe.py run fails outright — all 354 audio frames are discarded and no TranscriptionFrame ever arrives; a raw socket fed the same clip after `transcript.created` transcribes it correctly. Silent: no warning, no error. — *needs a call: a call on the fix — buffer the pre-ready audio and flush it on `transcript.created`, or hold the service's start until the session is ready; both change startup behavior*

**Medium**

- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default claude-sonnet-4-6 may be superseded by claude-sonnet-5-5 (GA 2026-09-28) (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — Sonnet 5.5 is Anthropic's newest Sonnet and the recommended replacement for the Sonnet 4.5 that retires on 2026-11-30. Latency through our class was measured on 2026-10-28 and is carried forward: TTFAT 462 ms against 777 ms trivial, 466 ms against 921 ms on a scheduling prompt, medians of 5. The leaderboard is unchanged this run: `claude-sonnet-5-5 (low)` 99.9% / 838 ms, and `claude-sonnet-4-6` is still only in the archived rows at 100.0% / 850 ms. — *needs a call: whether 99.9% against an archived, differently-judged 100.0% counts as parity, and whether to pair the bump with an effort level, since neither leaderboard row matches what Pipecat sends (between_tools at the default high effort)*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Replayed thinking blocks fail Anthropic's preserved-thinking prefix check (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — On Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2, released 2026-11-09, the check is enforced for every account, not only accounts created on or after 2026-08-31 as it is on Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5. The adapter round-trips thinking blocks with their signatures, and Pipecat is not append-only: `LLMContextSummarizer._apply_summary` calls `set_messages` to replace history, and `_should_inject_trailing_user_message` injects a "." user turn that never enters the context. The escape hatch, `thinking.block_binding.prefix_mismatch_behavior: "drop_block"` behind the `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01` beta, is reachable only by overriding `thinking` and the hard-coded `betas` list wholesale through `extra`. — *needs a call: whether Pipecat should opt into `drop_block` for Anthropic by default, expose the field, or make the summarizer and the trailing-user injection thinking-aware*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-09.md) — The anthropic extra pins <2, excluding the SDK major that Anthropic now ships (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `pyproject.toml` pins `anthropic>=0.49.0,<2`; the latest release is 2.7.0 (2026-11-09). SDK 2.0.0 sends API version `2026-10-21`, which removes `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` for every model — a 400 even at their default values — and drops them from the SDK types. `_apply_sampling_settings` sends them through `extra_body`, so they would reach the wire and fail on every model rather than only on Opus 4.7 and later. The migration also errors on `document` blocks in assistant messages and on `citations` in user messages. — *needs a call: whether to raise the ceiling to admit 2.x and make the sampling settings version-aware, or hold the pin until the sampling question below is settled*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Pipecat does not pick a low-latency effort level on Sonnet 4.6 (since 2026-08-25, 10 weeks) — `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` sends `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` on Sonnet 4.6, but effort is a separate control and the request omits it, so it runs at Anthropic's `high`. The effort page is unchanged: users are told to set effort explicitly on Sonnet 4.6 "to avoid unexpected latency", `medium` is the recommended default and `low` the latency-sensitive one. — *needs a call: whether the service should choose an effort level the way `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` already chooses a thinking mode, which changes output quality as well as latency*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Server Error messages and other unparsed message types become "Unknown message type" ErrorFrames (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — AssemblyAI documents an `Error` frame (`type`, `error_code`, `error`) sent immediately before close, with the close reason always "See Error message for details." — so the close code carries nothing; `_parse_message` knows Begin/Turn/SpeechStarted/Termination and raises on everything else, and SpeakerRevision, Heartbeat and LLMGatewayResponse take the same path — *needs a call: the Error case is a plain diagnostic fix, but SpeakerRevision corrects speaker labels on turns already pushed downstream — confirm whether to handle the two together or land the Error path alone*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — A model ID or parameter the server does not recognize is silently ignored, and nothing checks what actually ran (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — the streaming spec still tells clients to check Begin's `configuration.model` because unrecognized query parameters are ignored rather than rejected; `BeginMessage` parses only `id` and `expires_at`, and `U3_PRO_MODEL_PREFIXES` plus the deprecated `AssemblyAIConnectionParams.speech_model` Literal still offer `u3-rt-pro`, which the models page marks "do not use" — *needs a call: warning when the applied model differs from the requested one is new behavior on a hot path — decide whether to log it, and whether retired IDs should be rejected client-side*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Streaming PII redaction (redact_pii, redact_pii_policies, redact_pii_sub) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — three connect params plus `include_partial_turns`, which the server forces to false when redaction is on — *needs a call: redaction suppresses partial turns, so a stance is needed on what the service does with interim transcriptions when a user enables it*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Only agent_context and language_codes are applied live; every other setting change tears down the websocket (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — UpdateConfiguration accepts prompt, keyterms_prompt, min_turn_silence, max_turn_silence, vad_threshold, interruption_delay, agent_context, mode, end_of_turn_confidence_threshold, language_codes and session_heartbeat; HOT_UPDATABLE_SETTINGS lists two of the eleven — *needs a call: widening the set changes reconnect behavior mid-call, and the Pipecat turn mode rewrites min/max_turn_silence — needs a call on which fields are safe to hot-update*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Connect params inactivity_timeout, include_partial_turns, session_heartbeat and speaker_labels_revision_interval_ms have no Settings fields (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — each would be a one-line pass-through in `_build_ws_url`, but all four change session or frame behavior; inactivity_timeout also brings in the client-side KeepAlive message the service never sends — *needs a call: session_heartbeat and speaker_labels_revision_interval_ms are only useful once Heartbeat and SpeakerRevision are parsed, and include_partial_turns=false would starve the pipeline of interim frames — confirm which of the four the team wants exposed*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — AssemblyAISyncSTTService drops a turn on the responses AssemblyAI documents as retryable (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — the Sync error-handling page calls 429 and 503 transient ("retry after the `Retry-After` header, or a short backoff when absent") and 500/504 safe to retry once; `_transcribe` raises on any non-200 and `run_stt` turns that into an ErrorFrame, so the utterance is lost — *needs a call: a retry inside run_stt delays a live turn, so the retry budget and whether to honor Retry-After verbatim need a call*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — AssemblyAISyncSTTService posts the whole utterance after speech ends rather than streaming it to /v1/transcribe/live (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — the Sync API also exposes POST /v1/transcribe/live, a chunked multipart upload (config part first) that transcribes while the speaker is still talking; the service uses /v1/transcribe and /v1/warm only — *needs a call: streaming the body during the segment is a restructure of SegmentedSTTService's buffer-then-post flow, not a Settings field — needs a design call on whether the latency win is worth it*
- [assemblyai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Connect params inactivity_timeout and extend_biometric_consent have no Settings fields, and the cloned-voice consent expiry Begin reports is only logged — both are documented optional connect params; `Begin.configuration` carries `voice_expires_at` and `resolved_voice.extend_due_at`, which the service logs inside the whole configuration dump and never surfaces, so a cloned voice stops working with a 3012 `voice_expired` and no warning — *needs a call: extend_biometric_consent renews a biometric consent on the account rather than shaping a request, and it is connect-time only — confirm whether it belongs in Settings at all, or as a constructor argument*
- [asyncai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/asyncai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Server error frames are dropped by the websocket receive loop (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Async's error frames carry `error_code`, `message`, `extra` and `request_id` but no `context_id`, so `_receive_messages` sends them down its "unavailable context" branch and discards them. Reproduced this run through the ordinary mistake of constructing the service with no voice (the docs say "There is no default, so set one"): Async rejects the init message with `voice.id  UUID input should be a string, bytes or UUID object`, then rejects each following `sendText` with `voice  Field required` because the session was never initialized — three `BAD_REQUEST` frames, all discarded. The application gets one generic `ErrorFrame`, `TTS context <id> completed with no audio`, from `TTSService` ~3 s later at context completion, naming neither Async nor the reason. The same branch swallows the undocumented connect ack, `event: init_ack` with `request_id`, `context_restored` and a server-assigned `context_id`, which is why every healthy connect logs `Ignoring message from unavailable context: <uuid>`. — *needs a call: whether an `error_code` frame should push an ErrorFrame carrying Async's message and close the context, or only be logged now that the base class reports the silence*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-09.md) — The documented `latency` setting is rejected by the default model and by current Claude models (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `latency="optimized"` sends `performanceConfig`, which Bedrock still previews for Nova Pro, Llama 3.1 70B/405B and the retired Claude 3.5 Haiku only; the Settings docstring says only 'standard or optimized' — *needs a call: whether to name the supported models in the docstring, validate before sending, or drop the field as Bedrock narrows the preview*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default us.amazon.nova-lite-v1:0 is a generation behind Nova 2 Lite (GA 2025-12-02) (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — this run's TTFAT medians of 5 are 525 ms for the default against 588 ms for Nova 2 Lite, a 12% gap that falls back outside the 10% band after last run's 563/586; nova-lite-v1:0 is still ACTIVE with no EOL date — *needs a call: aiewf-eval still has no Amazon Nova row of any generation, so the benchmark condition for a default bump cannot be met yet*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Structured output is unreachable even though Bedrock made it GA for Claude on Converse (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Converse takes `outputConfig.textFormat` as `OutputFormat {type: json_schema, structure.jsonSchema}` and AWS announced structured outputs for Claude on 2026-10-18, documenting per-model support on the model cards; the service leaves `supports_response_schema` False, so `run_inference(response_schema=...)` logs "not supported and is ignored", and its docstring still asserts Bedrock's Converse API has no reply schema — *needs a call: whether to claim response-schema support and gate it per model through `model_supports_response_schema`, given that Nova rejects `outputConfig` outright and some cross-Region profiles do not support it either*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Bedrock's strict tool use (`toolSpec.strict`) is never requested (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — Bedrock's structured-outputs page documents `strict: true` on a tool definition as the way to get schema-valid tool names and arguments, and botocore's `ToolSpecification` carries the member; the Bedrock adapter emits `toolSpec` without it, so every function call is best-effort — *needs a call: whether strict tool use should be on for everyone, a Settings opt-in, or per tool — and what a model that rejects `strict` should do*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Bedrock Guardrails (`guardrailConfig`) are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — ConverseStream takes guardrailIdentifier/guardrailVersion/trace; the service never sends the block — *needs a call: no guardrail exists in the probe account to verify a pass-through against, and guardrail interventions arriving mid-stream need a stance on what the pipeline should see*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-09.md) — Agent runtimes using JWT/OAuth inbound auth cannot be invoked at all (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — The API reference still states plainly: "If you're integrating your agent with OAuth, you can't use the AWS SDK to call InvokeAgentRuntime. Instead, make a HTTPS request." A runtime created with `customJWTAuthorizer` accepts only a bearer token against `https://bedrock-agentcore.<region>.amazonaws.com/runtimes/<escaped-arn>/invocations?qualifier=...`. The processor invokes exclusively through aiobotocore (SigV4), so those runtimes are unreachable; a runtime supports either SigV4 or JWT, never both. — *needs a call: Whether a bearer-token HTTP invocation path belongs in this processor at all — it means a second transport (aiohttp) plus a token-refresh story, well past a pass-through setting.*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-09.md) — InvokeAgentRuntime's qualifier, runtimeUserId, contentType/accept and trace headers are not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — The call passes only `agentRuntimeArn` and `payload`, the two required members of the 16 the request has. `qualifier` selects a versioned endpoint instead of the default; `runtimeUserId` scopes a user for AgentCore Identity/Memory; `traceId`/`traceParent`/`traceState`/`baggage` carry distributed tracing into AgentCore observability; `accountId` addresses a runtime by agent ID instead of full ARN. The four `mcp*` members apply only to MCP-protocol runtimes. — *needs a call: The processor has no Settings class, so exposing these means either a new params object or several constructor arguments — a shape decision; runtimeUserId also needs the separate InvokeAgentRuntimeForUser IAM action, so it is not a free pass-through.*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-09.md) — RetryableConflictException (409) is not retried, unlike the other transient errors (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — The client is created with no `Config`, so botocore's default legacy retry mode applies: its `__default__` policies cover socket errors, 500/502/503/504 and the throttling family (429), up to 5 attempts. RetryableConflictException is 409 and its shape carries the modeled `retryable: {throttling: false}` trait that only standard/adaptive honour. The session guide pins the window down — while a session is being provisioned or torn down, a second operation on it returns 409 "Session operation in progress, please retry", to be retried with short exponential backoff. Untouched, the exception escapes the invocation and the turn is lost. — *needs a call: Whether to switch the client to retries mode standard — that changes default behaviour (added backoff inside a voice turn's latency budget), so it is a call, not a pass-through.*
- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — The first-generation code path still ships for a model Bedrock has removed (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `_is_first_generation_sonic_model()` gates the `ready.wav` assistant-response-trigger hack, `AWAIT_TRIGGER_ASSISTANT_RESPONSE_INSTRUCTION`, `trigger_assistant_response()` and the `endpointing_sensitivity` downgrade warning. The only model that selects it, `amazon.nova-sonic-v1:0`, is still rejected at connect with "The provided model identifier is invalid", so the path is unreachable in practice. — *needs a call: whether the first-generation path (and the bundled `ready.wav`) is deprecated for removal in 2.0.0 or deleted outright*
- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Authentication is limited to static IAM access keys; Bedrock API keys and the default credential chain are unreachable (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `access_key_id`/`secret_access_key`/`region` are required constructor arguments and `create_client()` hardcodes `StaticCredentialsResolver()` with a SigV4-only `auth_schemes` map, so an instance/task role, a named profile or a Bedrock API key cannot be used. The installed SDK 0.9.0 already lists `smithy.api#httpBearerAuth` alongside `aws.auth#sigv4` on the bidirectional-stream operation, and the Nova 2.5 Sonic model card still presents the API key (`AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`) as the getting-started credential. — *needs a call: a call on which auth modes to support (bearer API key, default credential chain) and how to keep the existing required arguments working*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Remaining StartStreamTranscription request parameters are unreachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Out of reach: `vocabulary-name` and `vocabulary-filter-name` (already parameters of `get_presigned_url`, never passed by the service), `vocabulary-filter-method`, `content-redaction-type` / `content-identification-type` / `pii-entity-types`, `language-model-name`, `show-speaker-label` and `enable-channel-identification` (private attributes fixed at False). — *needs a call: Which subset to expose first. None can be probed end-to-end without account-side resources (a custom vocabulary, a custom language model), so they cannot ride an automatic PR.*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-09.md) — A dropped connection starts a new transcription session rather than resuming (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — The presigned URL carries no `session-id` or `session-resume-window`, so the reconnect that `WebsocketSTTService` performs opens an unrelated stream and any in-flight segment is lost. AWS allows resuming within a window of 1-300 minutes. — *needs a call: Whether resume is worth the state it adds, given a voice turn is short and a lost partial is usually re-spoken.*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-09.md) — run_tts reads the whole AudioStream before yielding, so TTFB is full synthesis time (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — measured 2026-08-31 on generative: first chunk 182 ms vs full read 1193 ms; generative probes on 2026-10-28 landed at TTFB 723-834 ms against 226 ms for the standard default (330 ms this run). `AudioStream.iter_chunks()` is available on aiobotocore's stream — *needs a call: yielding partial audio changes when TTSAudioRawFrame first appears and how resampling is chunked; needs a call on reworking run_tts*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Polly's bidirectional streaming API (StartSpeechSynthesisStream, GA 2026-03-19) is not used (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — HTTP/2 stream that takes text incrementally and returns audio as synthesized, generative engine only; botocore ships the model but deletes the operation from the client (botocore/handlers.py remove_polly_start_speech_synthesis_stream), so it needs the smithy client aws_sdk_polly. Available in nine regions, most recently us-gov-west-1 (2026-10-22) — *needs a call: a streaming Polly class means a new class plus a new SDK dependency, the same shape as aws-nova-sonic's aws_sdk_bedrock_runtime extra — needs a product call*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The service leaves Engine unset, so Polly synthesizes with the standard engine (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — standard is Polly's oldest concatenative engine and its docs page still carries no retirement notice (re-read this run, 60 voices in 29 locales); neural measured at the same latency (TTFB 242 ms vs 239 ms, medians of 5, 2026-09-28) but neural costs $16 vs $4 per 1M characters — *needs a call: a default engine change raises every user's bill 4x and breaks voices that only exist as standard; needs a maintainer's call*
- [azure/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/image/2026-11-09.md) — Settings expose only `image_size`; the v1 images request documents quality, background, output_format and more (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Azure's v1 preview OpenAPI schema and the how-to page for `/images/generations` list `quality`, `background` (auto|transparent), `output_format` (png|jpeg; webp unsupported), `output_compression` (0-100, JPEG only), `moderation`, `n` (1-10), `style`, `response_format`, `user` and `stream`/`partial_images`. `AzureImageGenSettings` adds only `image_size` on top of the inherited `model`; `n` is hard-coded to 1. The parameter domains vary by model - gpt-image-2/2.5 take custom WIDTHxHEIGHT (edges multiples of 16, long edge <= 3840px) while gpt-image-1/-1.5/-mini take three fixed sizes, and quality is low|medium|high|xhigh|max|auto on 2.5 against low|medium|high elsewhere. — *needs a call: Which fields to expose depends on the endpoint rewrite above - the parameter set differs between the retired submit flow and the v1 endpoint, so adding fields now would encode the dead schema.*
- [azure/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/image/2026-11-09.md) — A non-2xx submit response raises `KeyError` instead of yielding an `ErrorFrame` (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `operation_location = submission.headers["operation-location"]` runs unguarded; the header is absent on any error response, so every call against the retired endpoint dies with an unhandled `KeyError` rather than an `ErrorFrame`. The code carries a comment acknowledging this. — *needs a call: Whether to patch the error path on its own or only as part of the endpoint rewrite - a standalone fix touches code that is due to be replaced.*
- [azure/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Azure's recommended Responses API has no Azure service class (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Azure's v1 API guidance still says "For Azure OpenAI models we recommend using the Responses API" (page unchanged, ms.date 2026-05-13), and OpenAIResponsesHttpLLMService already reaches it when base_url is the resource's /openai/v1/ endpoint (probed live on 2026-08-31, TTFAT 1157 ms vs 1860 ms for AzureLLMService chat completions on the same gpt-4.1 deployment). What is missing is an Azure entry point - no Entra ID token-provider path, and the WebSocket Responses class hardcodes wss://api.openai.com/v1/responses. — *needs a call: whether Azure gets a Responses subclass plus docs, or users are simply pointed at OpenAIResponsesHttpLLMService with base_url*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — CLI scaffold passes `input_audio_transcription=` to SessionProperties, a field the event model no longer has (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `SessionProperties(input_audio_transcription=InputAudioTranscription(model="whisper-1"))` dumps to `{'type': 'realtime'}` — the field is not in `SessionProperties.model_fields` and pydantic drops the kwarg — so scaffolded Azure bots run with no user transcription. The shape the model accepts is `audio=AudioConfiguration(input=AudioInput(transcription=...))`, as `examples/realtime/realtime-azure.py` uses. On Azure the transcription `model` must also name a transcription deployment, not the literal `whisper-1`. — *needs a call: the scaffold needs a transcription deployment name from somewhere, so fixing the shape alone still leaves a literal that only works by coincidence*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Reasoning settings are stripped for Azure deployments of gpt-realtime-2/2.1 because the check reads the deployment name (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `OpenAIRealtimeLLMService._strip_unsupported_reasoning` keeps `reasoning` only when `settings.model` contains one of `_REASONING_CAPABLE_MODEL_SUBSTRINGS` (`gpt-realtime-2`, `gpt-realtime-2.1`, `openai/realtime/llm.py:860`).
