On Azure that string is the deployment name, so a reasoning-capable deployment called anything else silently loses `Reasoning(effort=...)` (with a warning). `gpt-realtime-2.1` is GA on Azure, so reasoning-capable deployments are production, not preview. — *needs a call: how Azure should decide reasoning capability when the model name is opaque — trust the user (no stripping on Azure) or add an explicit opt-in*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Azure's newest STT models are not reachable; the recognizer always runs the base streaming model (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Speech SDK 1.52 added streaming model selection - Python sets `speech_config.model = "mai-transcribe-2-streaming"` before constructing the recognizer. The probe environment now runs SDK 1.53.0, where `SpeechConfig.model` exists, but the model is still public preview (doc reviewed 2026-10-07) and served only from swedencentral/centralus/eastus2/southeastasia; our probe credential's region is not one of them, so the path is still unverified. `AzureSTTSettings` already inherits `model` from `STTSettings` and this class ignores it, so a user who sets it today gets silence — *needs a call: `SpeechConfig.model` does not exist below SDK 1.52 while the `azure` extra still allows 1.47, so wiring the inherited `model` field means raising the floor or guarding on the attribute - plus a stance on shipping a preview model path we cannot probe from this account's region.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Explicit audio-boundary commit (`PushAudioInputStream.commit()`) is not used (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — preview feature Azure documents for "turn-based voice agents" - `set_service_property("setfeature", "forcecommit", ServicePropertyChannel.UriQueryParameter)` on the config, `pushStream.commit()` after the last audio of a turn, and `result.commit_token` on the matching `recognized` event. It would finalize a turn on the user-stopped-speaking signal instead of waiting out `Speech_SegmentationSilenceTimeoutMs`. Python has had `commit()` since 1.52 (present on the installed 1.53.0); the format limits (PCM/A-law/mu-law/G.711, no Microsoft Audio Stack) are all satisfied here — *needs a call: not a pass-through field - it needs the service to observe turn-stop and call `commit()`, plus the same SDK floor question; decide whether a preview latency path belongs in the service.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Semantic segmentation (`Speech_SegmentationStrategy="Semantic"`) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — one more `set_property` pass-through alongside the merged `segmentation_silence_timeout_ms`; segments on sentence-ending punctuation instead of silence. Needs SDK 1.41+, which our floor already satisfies. The documented loss of confidence scores and NBest lists costs this class nothing - it reads only `result.text` — *needs a call: Azure scopes Semantic to continuous recognition for dictation and captioning rather than interactive agents, and it is not offered in every locale - exposing it needs a stance plus a latency measurement on agent-length turns.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Automatic language detection is not supported; the recognizer is always pinned to one locale (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Azure needs `AutoDetectSourceLanguageConfig` passed to the `SpeechRecognizer` constructor (open-range form covers the multilingual set); `_on_handle_recognized` already reads `result.language`, so per-utterance language would flow through. It is also the gate on multilingual post-stream refinement, which is GA since October and needs the open-range config in addition to `PostRefinement` — *needs a call: a second recognizer construction path plus a stance on what `Settings.language` means when detection is on - bigger than a pass-through field.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Phrase lists (keyword/entity biasing) are not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `PhraseListGrammar.from_recognizer(...)` boosts names and domain terms and is supported on real-time transcription, including both post-stream refinement modes; the cap is 2,000 phrases generally and 200 phrases / 3,200 characters on the multilingual refinement path, and `set_weight` takes 0.0-2.0 — *needs a call: it attaches to the recognizer, not the config, so it has to be re-applied on every reconnect and cleared on update - decide whether that lives in `_connect` or a dedicated setting handler.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-09.md) — MAI voices emit no word boundaries, so AzureTTSService produces audio but no TTSTextFrame (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `AzureTTSService` sets `push_text_frames=False` and derives text frames from Azure's word boundary events. Re-probed today with Pipecat's exact SSML: `en-US-Harper:MAI-Voice-2.1-Flash` returned 0 word boundaries where `en-US-SaraNeural`, `en-US-Ava:DragonHDLatestNeural` and `en-US-Ava:DragonHDOmniLatestNeural` each returned 11. Only the MAI family is affected, and it is the one Microsoft points at real-time voice agents. — *needs a call: a fallback (push the aggregated sentence when a synthesis completes with no word boundary) changes timing semantics for every voice — someone has to decide whether that fallback is unconditional or keyed to the voice family.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Custom voice deployments are unreachable; the TTS classes have no endpoint_id (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `AzureSTTService` already takes `endpoint_id` and sets `SpeechConfig.endpoint_id`; the TTS classes only take `private_endpoint`, which is Private Link/custom domain, not a fine-tuned voice deployment. — *needs a call: it is a constructor argument rather than a runtime setting, so it needs a maintainer's call on the shape (and on whether it pairs with `voice` validation).*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The `azure` extra floor (`azure-cognitiveservices-speech>=1.47.0`) predates TTS fixes in 1.50 through 1.53 (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — 1.50 fixed a TTS connection race condition, 1.51 fixed TTS token credential errors on the REST backend, 1.51.2 fixed a SIGABRT crash during speech synthesis plus a Python AudioDataStream memory leak, 1.52 fixed two TTS crashes with compressed audio, and 1.53 (2026-10-21) fixes another; 1.48.2 carries the CRL fix Microsoft required for Linux/Android by 2026-07-01. Fresh resolves get 1.53.0, so only pinned-old installs are exposed. Shared with azure/stt, which raised the same item. — *needs a call: whether provider-watch may move dependency floors, or whether that is a maintainer-owned change.*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `reasoning_effort` and `parallel_tool_calls` session properties are unreachable (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — Both are documented top-level fields of RealtimeRequestSession. `SessionProperties` is a Pydantic model with the default `extra="ignore"`, so `SessionProperties(reasoning_effort="low", parallel_tool_calls=False)` validates and then serialises to nothing. The reference documents reasoning_effort values none/minimal/low/medium/high/xhigh, which matter for the GPT-5 and GPT-6 models Voice Live now offers. — *needs a call: a probe against a live Voice Live session to confirm the fields are accepted, which needs a Foundry resource credential*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `InputAudioNoiseReduction.type` rejects the documented headset and personal suppression modes — api-version 2026-07-15 documents four noise-reduction models: RealtimeOpenAINoiseReduction (near_field/far_field), RealtimeAzureDeepNoiseSuppression, RealtimeAzureDeepNoiseSuppressionHeadset (azure_deep_noise_suppression_headset) and RealtimeAzurePersonalNoiseSuppression (azure_personal_noise_suppression). Our Literal allows only the first three values, so the two new modes raise a ValidationError before a request is built. Widening the Literal is a one-line change. — *needs a call: the docs say both modes require deployment support, and the change needs a live session to confirm the service accepts them; no Voice Live credential here.*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Interim responses (`interim-response`) cannot be configured (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — Voice Live can speak filler while a tool runs or latency exceeds a threshold, via a static (`static-interim-response`) or LLM-generated (`llm-interim-response`) config on the session, and per-response via `interim_response` on response options. Pipecat has no equivalent for a realtime service, and the session key is hyphenated so it needs a field alias. — *needs a call: whether Pipecat should surface a provider-side filler mechanism at all, given pipelines usually handle this themselves*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `EndOfUtteranceDetection.threshold` does not match the documented `threshold_level` (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — RealtimeEOUDetection documents `model` (semantic_detection_v1, _v1_en, _v1_multilingual), `threshold_level` (low/medium/high/default) and `timeout_ms`. The events model exposes `threshold: float` instead, so the documented knob is unreachable and the float is sent under a name the schema does not list. — *needs a call: whether `threshold` is still accepted by the service (a rename would be a breaking change for anyone setting it), which needs a live session to check*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Foundry Agent Service sessions are not supported (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — An agent-backed session connects with `agent_id` and `project_id` query parameters instead of `model`; `_connect` always sends `model`. Agents also reject `instructions`, which the service always syncs into the session. — *needs a call: new constructor arguments and a branch in the session payload, so larger than a settings pass-through; worth deciding whether agent-backed Voice Live is in scope*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Transport input sample rates other than 16000/24000 are forwarded unchanged (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — `_ensure_audio_config` forces `input_audio_format` to pcm16 and sets `input_audio_sampling_rate` to the transport's rate; the how-to documents 16000 and 24000 as the supported PCM values (the reference notes 8000 only as the default for g711, which this service never sends). An 8 kHz telephony transport therefore configures an undocumented rate, where the output path already falls back to 24 kHz with a warning. — *needs a call: confirmation that Voice Live rejects (rather than resamples) 8000 Hz PCM input before adding a resample-or-warn path*
- [baseten/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/baseten/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default zai-org/GLM-5.3 is three times slower to first answer token than Baseten models that score higher on aiewf-eval (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — Same item as last run, reattributed: the reasoning penalty is gone (thinking 0 ms on a trivial prompt, 151 ms on a reasoning one) but the absolute latency is not. Interleaved --repeat 5 today puts GLM-5.3 at TTFAT 1745 ms median against 521 ms for deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, and aiewf-eval's aiwf_medium_context now ranks deepseek-v4-flash-0731 (high, Baseten) 98.6% / 773 ms P50 above glm-5.3 (low, Z.AI) 97.7% / 2376 ms. No GLM successor exists, so the choice is cross-family. — *needs a call: Baseten designates no successor to GLM-5.3, so moving the default to the DeepSeek family is a deliberate choice about which open-weights model Pipecat recommends, not a mechanical bump.*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-09.md) — MARS 8.1 beta models are missing from MODEL_SAMPLE_RATES, so their 48 kHz audio is labelled 22050 Hz (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `mars-8.1-flash-beta` and `mars-8.1-pro-beta` are in the SDK's speech_model literal and are the endpoint's documented default; `MODEL_SAMPLE_RATES.get(model, 22050)` falls back to 22050 for both, and the docs list both at 48 kHz. Camb also lists mars-flash as 22.05 kHz/48 kHz, which the single table entry cannot express. — *needs a call: a live probe to confirm the beta models' actual stream rate, which CAMB_API_KEY currently blocks; also a call on whether beta models belong in the table at all*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Camb's `voice_settings` (speaking_rate, maintain_source_accent, enhance_reference_audio_quality) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `StreamTtsVoiceSettings` is a documented parameter of the streaming TTS endpoint the service calls; `speaking_rate` (default 1) is documented as the only way to adjust pacing, since the endpoint has no duration parameter. `run_tts` never builds the object. — *needs a call: a probe with the field set, which the rejected credential blocks; and a call on whether to expose speaking_rate alone or the whole nested object*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The constructor's `sample_rate` is never sent to Camb, so a user-chosen rate mislabels the audio (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `output_configuration` carries a documented `sample_rate` field, but the service only sets `format="pcm_s16le"`. A caller passing `sample_rate=` gets a warning and frames tagged at their rate while the stream stays at the model's native rate. The same object's `apply_enhancement` is also unreachable. — *needs a call: a call on whether to request the rate from Camb or keep the warning-only behaviour, plus a live probe of a non-native rate*
- [cartesia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Default ink-whisper is Cartesia's older STT model; ink-2 is the stable recommended one (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — latency is unchanged for a fifth run - TTFS 749 ms for ink-2 against 266 ms for ink-whisper, in line with the 523/239 median-of-5 comparison from 2026-10-12, so the default-bump latency test still fails. ink-2 stays five languages (en/es/fr/hi/ja) while ink-whisper covers 100; the `/stt/websocket` reference still scopes `language` to ink-whisper, leaving non-English ink-2 through this class undocumented. ink-whisper is still Stable on the older-models page with no sunset date. — *needs a call: whether the manual-endpoint service should follow ink-2 and accept a 2-3x TTFS regression, or stay on ink-whisper and leave ink-2 to CartesiaTurnsSTTService*
- [cartesia/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/tts/2026-11-09.md) — A dict-valued `generation_config` crashes synthesis (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `CartesiaTTSSettings.from_mapping` leaves the nested value a plain dict and both services call `.model_dump()` on it, so the turn errors out with no audio; reproduced again this run from `probe.py --setting generation_config={"emotion":"calm"}`; fish, inworld, google and anthropic override `from_mapping` for exactly this — *needs a call: whether the dict path should coerce nested values into `GenerationConfig` (and what to do with unknown keys) or reject them outright*
- [cartesia/turns-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/turns-stt/2026-11-09.md) — `ink-2` transcribes five languages but the service stamps every frame `Language.EN` (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — Cartesia made `ink-2` multilingual (en, fr, hi, ja, es, auto-detected) on 2026-09-17, so this affects the default model; the constructor pins `self._language = Language.EN` and reports a `language` settings update as unhandled, so a Spanish turn reaches the LLM tagged English, and the class docstring, the Settings docstring and our docs page all still say the family is English-only at launch — *needs a call: the turn events still carry no detected language, so labeling needs a call — let the user declare a `language`, or leave frames unlabeled*
- [cerebras/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cerebras/llm/2026-11-09.md) — qwen-3.8-27b is the only alternative on the public tier and is unbenchmarked for voice (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — Unchanged this run. aiewf-eval still has no row for `gpt-oss-120b` (the Groq row was archived 2026-10-17; last published 86.3% pass / 98 ms P50 with an unresolved cohort mapping), and the qwen rows still read 96.0% / 649 ms (thinking off, FP8, Baseten) and 95.1% / 101 ms (thinking off, NVFP4, local). Cerebras positions qwen-3.8-27b as the Shared Inference replacement for the retired `gemma-4-31b`, not for `gpt-oss-120b`, which is not deprecated and is still the named replacement for most earlier retirements. It works as-is through the thin wrapper (no allowlist); this run's single-turn probe put it at TTFAT 301 ms against the default's 154 ms, consistent with the previous run's finding that the shared tier is too noisy to rank the two. — *needs a call: A call on whether a candidate may be bumped over a default whose benchmark row has been withdrawn, given that Cerebras names qwen-3.8-27b as gemma-4-31b's replacement rather than gpt-oss-120b's - and on whether a bump would have to pin `reasoning_effort="none"`, since Cerebras runs qwen-3.8-27b at reasoning `high` by default while every benchmarked row is thinking-off.*
- [crusoe/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/crusoe/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default openai/gpt-oss-120b is below the Crusoe-hosted field on aiewf-eval aiwf_medium_context (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — Crusoe deprecated `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro` and `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash` on 2026-11-02, so last run's shortlist — the only two leaderboard rows that mapped to Crusoe-served checkpoints — is void. Crusoe's own migration target for them is `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash` (HF `DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017`), which aiewf-eval has not benchmarked. Crusoe-hosted rows still on the board: glm-5.3 (low reasoning) 97.7% / 2376 ms, gemma-4-31b-it (thinking off) 96.4% / 497 ms, qwen3.8-27b (thinking off, FP8) 96.0% / 649 ms, nemotron-3.5-lightning (thinking on, NVFP4) 83.9% / 1470 ms. The default itself still has no row under the current Opus 5.5 judge — the groq gpt-oss-120b row stays archived at a pre-rejudge 86.3%. gpt-oss-120b remains listed and unmarked, so nothing forces a move; dropped from high to medium because the actionable candidates were retired out from under the item. — *needs a call: whether to wait for an aiewf-eval row on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash or pick from the three benchmarked Crusoe-hosted models despite the default having only an archived pre-rejudge score — and a working CRUSOE_API_KEY either way, since no candidate can be probed*
- [crusoe/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/crusoe/llm/2026-11-09.md) — CRUSOE_API_KEY is rejected with HTTP 401, so the unit has gone unprobed for five runs (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — both /v1/models and a chat completion answer 401; the base_url and request shape match Crusoe's documented endpoint, so this is a credential problem on our side, not a service defect — *needs a call: someone with Crusoe Intelligence Foundry access to reissue the key in the provider-watch secret store, or a decision to mark the unit docs-only until one exists*
- [deepgram/flux-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-stt/2026-11-09.md) — The `sagemaker` extra pins `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2<0.10`, three minors behind 0.12.0 (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — latest is still 0.12.0 (2026-10-08); the pin excludes 0.10/0.11/0.12, so an app on a current AWS preview SDK cannot resolve `pipecat-ai[deepgram,sagemaker]`. AWS publishes no release notes for the package, and `pipecat.services.aws.sagemaker.bidi_client` still imports the deprecated `SageMakerRuntimeHTTP2Client` alias. — *needs a call: the pin is shared by every SageMaker service, not just Flux, and the SDK is a 0.x preview with no changelog — widening it needs someone to run the AWS BiDi services against 0.12.0*
- [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-09.md) — Flux's inline IPA pronunciation is not emitted; `format_pronunciation` returns None (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — Still Early Access. Flux streaming accepts the exact markup `format_deepgram_pronunciation` already builds for Aura-2, so support is the Aura-2 `format_pronunciation` override plus a `supports_pronunciations` gate on `speed`. The control matrix still limits Flux controls to English, so a gate would have to account for the 41 non-English languages. — *needs a call: whether to emit an Early-Access control whose misuse is fatal — a pronunciation on a connection with `speed` set closes the session with `DATA-0002`, and a `Configure` speed over a queued pronunciation turn is `CONTROL_COMBINATION_INVALID`*
- [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-09.md) — `Interrupt` is sent without `playback_offset`, so Flux never reports `text_spoken` / `text_remaining` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Deepgram's documented barge-in pattern is Interrupt + `playback_offset: {type: time_ms, value: <session ms played>}`; the server then returns the exact text the user heard so the LLM context can be corrected. `on_audio_context_interrupted` sends a bare `{"type": "Interrupt"}`, so both fields are omitted and `audio_played_ms` falls back to the server's generated-audio total — which the code comment already notes. — *needs a call: whether Pipecat can hand the service a session-cumulative played-milliseconds counter, and whether Flux's `text_spoken` should override the assistant aggregator's own account of what was spoken*
- [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-09.md) — The `sagemaker` extra pins `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2<0.10` and the BiDi client imports a deprecated alias (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — Latest is still 0.12.0 (2026-10-08); the pin stops at 0.9.0. On 0.9.0 the import in `src/pipecat/services/aws/sagemaker/bidi_client.py` warns that `SageMakerRuntimeHTTP2Client` is a deprecated alias for `AsyncSageMakerRuntimeHTTP2Client`, and the Flux SageMaker tests emit that warning on every run. The SDK is pre-1.0 and documents breaking changes before then. — *needs a call: someone with a deployed SageMaker endpoint to verify the BiDi client against 0.12 before widening the pin and switching to the async client name*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Keyterm changes reconnect the WebSocket instead of using Deepgram's `/v1/listen` `Configure` message (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — The blockers this item carried last run are gone: deepgram-sdk 7.13.0 added `send_configure(ListenV1Configure(keyterms=..., features=...))` to the Listen v1 socket client, 7.14.0 added typed `ListenV1ConfigureSuccess`/`ListenV1ConfigureFailure`, and Deepgram extended mid-stream keyterm updates to the EU, AU and IN endpoints on 2026-10-19. `DeepgramSTTService._update_settings` still reconnects on any change, so updating `keyterm` (or `numerals`, which `features` also toggles) drops and re-establishes the connection and loses the audio buffered across it. Verified present on the installed 7.17.0. — *needs a call: whether `_update_settings` should route keyterm and numerals changes through `Configure` and reconnect only for the rest, and what to do with a `ConfigureFailure`*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Deepgram's `UtteranceEnd`, `SpeechStarted` and `Configure` reply messages are dropped, so the `utterance_end_ms` Settings field has no observable effect (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_on_message` handles only `ListenV1Results`; the SDK also exports `ListenV1UtteranceEnd`, `ListenV1SpeechStarted`, `ListenV1Metadata`, `ListenV1Error` and (since 7.14.0) `ListenV1ConfigureSuccess`/`ListenV1ConfigureFailure`. Setting `utterance_end_ms` therefore changes the request but nothing downstream — Pipecat ends turns from VAD plus `Finalize` instead — and any Configure work depends on reading the reply. — *needs a call: whether Deepgram's endpointing signals should drive turn-taking at all now that VAD plus `Finalize` does, or whether the dead knob should just be documented as inert*
- [deepgram/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The `sagemaker` extra pins `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2<0.10`, three releases behind the published 0.12.0 (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — pyproject still pins `>=0.6.0,<0.10`; PyPI is still at 0.12.0 (2026-10-08), with 0.10.0 and 0.11.0 in between. The package calls itself experimental with breaking changes possible before 1.0.0, so the ceiling reads as deliberate. `DeepgramSageMakerTTSService` imports only `ResponseStreamEventPayloadPart`, but the shared `SageMakerBidiClient` uses more of the surface, and the same pin gates the Flux SageMaker TTS and STT classes. — *needs a call: whether to raise the ceiling, which needs a deployed SageMaker endpoint to verify 0.12.0 against — and a decision on whether this belongs to the Deepgram units or to a single AWS SageMaker owner*
- [deepgram/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `tag`, Deepgram's usage-reporting label, is a constructor argument on the Flux TTS services but not on the Aura ones (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — documented as a query parameter in the REST `/v1/speak` reference (a string or list); `DeepgramFluxTTSService` and the Flux STT services already take a `tag` list and append one `tag=` per value. The Aura WebSocket reference still lists only `model`, `encoding`, `sample_rate`, `speed` and `mip_opt_out`, and an earlier probe showed the WebSocket opens happily with `tag=` — but equally happily with an invented parameter name, so tolerating it proves nothing about whether it is recorded. — *needs a call: whether Aura should grow the constructor argument, and whether Deepgram records a WebSocket `tag` in usage reporting at all (only the usage report can say)*
- [deepgram/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Settings `extra` never reaches the request in either Aura class, so any parameter without a declared field is silently dropped (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_connect_websocket` and `run_tts` build their query from named fields only, so `Settings(extra={...})` is accepted, stored and ignored — there is no escape hatch for `tag`, `callback`, `bit_rate` or anything Deepgram adds next. The WebSocket endpoint ignores unknown query keys rather than rejecting them, so forwarding `extra` would be safe but would also fail silently on a typo. The STT service has the mirror-image bug (it forwards `extra` into an SDK call that rejects undeclared keys). — *needs a call: whether Deepgram TTS should forward undeclared keys to the query string unvalidated, or reject them at construction instead of dropping them*
- [deepseek/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepseek/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `reasoning_effort` (none/low/high/max) has no Settings field (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the only way to ask for a cheaper reasoning pass than DeepSeek's default `high`; it reaches the API today only as `extra={"reasoning_effort": "low"}`. A typed field would collide with the shipped `thinking` default - the API documents `thinking` as taking precedence over `reasoning_effort`, and last run's direct check confirmed `thinking:disabled` yields 0 reasoning tokens alongside `reasoning_effort:high`, so the field would silently do nothing unless the user also changes `thinking` — *needs a call: whether a typed field is worth adding given it only takes effect once `thinking` is enabled or cleared, or whether `extra` remains the answer*
- [deepseek/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepseek/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `LLMSettings.use_provider_thinking_default` has no effect on DeepSeekLLMService — the service bakes `thinking=DeepSeekThinkingConfig(type="disabled")` into its constructor defaults instead of applying it at request-build time through `LLMService._apply_thinking_default`, as Anthropic, Google, OpenAI Responses and Perplexity do. A `BackendLLMWorker`, which sets `use_provider_thinking_default=True`, therefore still gets thinking disabled, and no per-model log line says which setting was sent — *needs a call: whether to move the disabled default into a `_maybe_apply_thinking_default` hook, which changes what `service.settings.thinking` reads back as and what an explicit `thinking=None` means*
- [elevenlabs/dialogue-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/dialogue-tts/2026-11-09.md) — `pronunciation_dictionary_locators` cannot be sent on a Text-to-Dialogue context (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the endpoint accepts them on a context's first message; ElevenLabsTTSService already takes them as a constructor argument, this class does not — *needs a call: a constructor argument to mirror the sibling class, plus a stance on word timestamps — Text-to-Dialogue documents `normalized_alignment` as unused, so dictionary substitutions would shift every timestamp in the turn*
- [elevenlabs/dialogue-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/dialogue-tts/2026-11-09.md) — Only one voice per context is registered, so multi-speaker dialogue is unreachable (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the websocket reference now states the limits — eleven_v3 takes up to 10 voices per context, eleven_v3_conversational and eleven_v4 exactly one, with eleven_v4_turbo unnamed; the 2026-10-12 probe had eleven_v4_turbo accept a two-voice context — *needs a call: whether a Pipecat TTS service should express per-utterance speakers at all, and through what API*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Entity detection and redaction are not reachable from either Settings class (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `entity_detection` / `entity_redaction` / `entity_redaction_mode` on batch; realtime gained `entity_redaction` and `entity_redaction_mode` on 2026-11-02 and now answers with both `committed_transcript_entities` and `redacted_transcript`, which `_process_response` treats as unknown — *needs a call: a stance on the response side — entities and redacted text need somewhere to go beyond `TranscriptionFrame.result`, and ElevenLabs charges a surcharge*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Zero-retention mode (`enable_logging=false`) cannot be set on ElevenLabsSTTService (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the realtime class takes `enable_logging` as a constructor argument; the batch endpoint takes the same query parameter (default true) but the batch class never sends it, so enterprise customers cannot opt out of log storage on it — *needs a call: whether to match the realtime class's constructor argument or make it a Settings field on both*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The batch service always uploads WAV, never `file_format=pcm_s16le_16` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — ElevenLabs documents lower latency for raw 16 kHz mono PCM than for an encoded waveform; SegmentedSTTService hands `run_stt` a WAV, so using it means reaching past that conversion — *needs a call: whether the segmented base class should expose the raw PCM segment, and whether the saving is worth measuring first*
- [elevenlabs/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `clone_strength` is not reachable from Settings on either class — added to TTS `voice_settings` on 2026-10-26; controls how closely a v4 Professional Voice Clone follows its training samples (0-1, ElevenLabs recommends 0), and since 2026-11-02 v4 PVC fine-tuning is open to all workspaces. ElevenLabsDialogueTTSService already has it as a Settings field with a v4-only guard (commit 352a543ad); the two classes here share `build_elevenlabs_voice_settings`, whose key list is hard-coded — *needs a call: the field is v4-only and the WebSocket endpoint serves no v4 model and does not document `clone_strength` in its RealtimeVoiceSettings, so adding it means either an HTTP-only field or a shared builder that knows which class and model it is building for*
- [fal/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fal/image/2026-11-09.md) — `format` is SDXL's field name and is silently ignored on fal's other image endpoints (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — re-checked this run — `fal-ai/nano-banana-2`, `fal-ai/z-image/turbo`, `fal-ai/gpt-image-1.5` and `fal-ai/flux/schnell` all name the field `output_format`, and `fal-ai/bytedance/seedream/v4.5/text-to-image` has no format field at all; `num_inference_steps` is absent from nano-banana-2, gpt-image-1.5 and seedream 4.5; fal ignores the unknown fields, so requests still return 200 with the endpoint's own defaults — *needs a call: whether the service should map the field per endpoint, add a parallel `output_format` setting, or stay SDXL-shaped and say so in the docstring*
- [fireworks/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fireworks/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `build_chat_completion_params` drops `seed` and `max_completion_tokens`, which Fireworks now supports (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the override was written when Fireworks rejected these parameters; the current chat-completions spec documents both (plus `service_tier`), and a probe with all three set via `extra` succeeds. Set through `Settings`, they are silently dropped. — *needs a call: dropping the override makes two previously ignored Settings fields start taking effect for every Fireworks user, which is more than a value change*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `drama-3` performs directions, sounds and paired tags but gets no expression adapter — `get_expression_adapter()` returns the S2 adapter only for models starting with `s2`, so `drama-3` falls back to `BaseExpressionAdapter` and performs nothing; drama-3's markup is its own — plain-language directions in square brackets, bracketed sounds and pauses, and seven paired tags (`<whisper>`, `<emphasis>`, `<soft>`, `<fast>`, `<slow>`, `<stress>`, `<forceful>`) that wrap the words they shape — *needs a call: whether drama-3 earns a second Fish adapter plus its own example and manifest entry, given the paired-tag syntax differs from S2's bracket tags*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Pronunciation dictionaries are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — `TTSRequest.pronunciation_dictionary` takes up to 3 dictionaries in either a reference form (`{id, version}`) or an inline form (`{items: [{key, value, case_sensitive?}]}`), mutually exclusive in one request; the public spec now also documents the `/v1/pronunciation-dictionaries` CRUD and version endpoints that mint the reference IDs — *needs a call: a stance on which form Settings should carry, or whether to model both, since they cannot both be set in one request*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Fish's word-timestamp WebSocket endpoint is not used, so the service emits no word timestamps (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `wss://api.fish.audio/v1/tts/live/with-timestamp` takes the same start request and returns `audio` events carrying a cumulative `alignment.segments` snapshot per `chunk_seq` plus `chunk_audio_offset_sec`; `TTSService.add_word_timestamps` is the hook, but the service connects to the plain `/v1/tts/live` and never parses alignment — *needs a call: whether word timestamps are worth a second endpoint and cumulative-alignment bookkeeping, or whether the service should switch to the timestamped endpoint outright*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Inline voice references and multi-speaker synthesis are not expressible (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the start request takes `references` (inline zero-shot cloning) and accepts an array of model IDs in `reference_id` for multi-speaker dialogue with `<|speaker:N|>` text tags on the S2 family and `drama-3`; `Settings.voice` is a single string and nothing sends `references` — *needs a call: a stance on multi-speaker TTS — whether `voice` may hold a list and where reference audio bytes belong in Settings*
- [funasr/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/funasr/stt/2026-11-09.md) — SenseVoice emotion and audio-event markers reach the LLM as emoji in the transcript (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `rich_transcription_postprocess` in funasr 1.4.23 still maps `<|HAPPY|>`/`<|Laughter|>`/`<|BGM|>`/`<|Applause|>` to emoji via `emo_dict`/`event_dict`, so `TranscriptionFrame.text` can be `🎼Play some music.` and flows into the context and back through TTS. A setting to drop non-speech markers, or stripping them before the frame, would fix it. — *needs a call: whether non-speech markers should be stripped by default or kept behind an opt-in, since the emotion/event labels are the reason some users pick SenseVoice*
- [funasr/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/funasr/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Models can only be downloaded from ModelScope; FunASR's `hub="hf"` is unreachable (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `AutoModel` documents `hub="ms"|"hf"` (default `ms`, propagated to VAD/punc/speaker sub-models) alongside `model_revision`, `ncpu` and `trust_remote_code`; the service passes only `model`, `device` and `disable_update`, so users restricted to HuggingFace mirrors cannot load a model. — *needs a call: whether this is a constructor argument next to `device` or a Settings field, given the model is loaded once in `__init__`*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `Settings(language=...)` is never sent to Gladia, so it silently does nothing (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_prepare_settings` builds the init body from `language_config` only; `s.language` is converted by the base class and then dropped, with no warning. Re-probed this run: `--setting language=fr` on an English clip returned the English transcript unchanged. docs.pipecat.ai now says the field is "Not sent by this service, so it has no effect" (it documented it as a working setting last run), which is why this is no longer high. — *needs a call: a stance on precedence — whether `language` should populate `language_config.languages` when `language_config` is also set, or be rejected/warned instead*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The shipped GLADIA_TTFS_P99 (1.49 s) only holds when the client is near the processing region (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `region` defaults to Gladia's eu-west. From the US runner, TTFS (speech end → final transcript, the service's own TTFB metric) was 1255 ms median of 3 (1247–1297 ms) in eu-west against 782 ms median of 3 (474–786 ms) with `region=us-west`. Much closer than the 8653 ms and 4871 ms the last two runs measured in eu-west, so the far-region penalty is route-dependent, but it still crossed 1.49 s on two of three runs. Turn stop strategies budget their wait from `ttfs_p99_latency`. — *needs a call: whether to say this in the `region` / `ttfs_p99_latency` docstrings (and which region the published constant was measured in), or to leave it to the stt-benchmark*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-09.md) — Gemini's `custom_vocabulary` is not reachable from Settings; the service sends the deprecated `adaptation_phrases` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `custom_vocabulary` (up to 1000 terms) is the only biasing field the Live transcription docs document; `adaptation_phrases` is still marked "Deprecated" in google-genai 3.0.0, so nothing breaks yet. The SDK floor is not a blocker — the lock resolves 2.34.0. — *needs a call: whether to rename `adaptation_phrases` with a deprecation, add `custom_vocabulary` alongside it, or keep the Settings name and just send `custom_vocabulary` on the wire*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-09.md) — google-genai 3.0.0 is outside the google extra's `<3` pin, and its new v1 default would change the Live endpoint this service connects to — 3.0.0 (2026-11-06) defaults the Gemini Developer API to `v1` instead of `v1beta` and switches the HTTP client to httpx2. `live.connect()` builds the BidiGenerateContent websocket URI from the client's `api_version`, and `update_google_client_http_options` sets no version, so a pin bump silently moves this service off v1beta. 3.0.0 also adds `source_text` to `AudioTranscriptionConfig` (voice-driven editing of an existing text, SMART mode only). — *needs a call: the pin covers every Google service, not just STT; someone has to decide whether to raise it to `<4` and whether to pin `api_version` explicitly so the Live endpoint does not move*
- [google/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/image/2026-11-09.md) — google-genai 3.0.0 is outside our <3 pin — released 2026-11-06; breaking changes are repo-wide rather than image-specific — Gemini Developer API clients default to API version v1 instead of v1beta, and httpx2 replaces httpx as the default HTTP client; the pin is shared by every service in the google extra — *needs a call: a decision on raising the shared pin, which has to be taken across all google services at once rather than per unit*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-09.md) — The gemini-3.6-flash default is faster but scores lower than gemini-3.8-flash, which the GoogleInteractionsVertexLLMService subclass defaults to — Interleaved medians of 5 through this class, trivial prompt: gemini-3.6-flash TTFAT 988 ms (spread 764-1044 ms) against 1732 ms for gemini-3.8-flash (spread 1414-2739 ms), thinking_ms 2-3 and 5/5 ok for both. aiewf-eval medium-context has gemini-3.8-flash (low) at 99.9% / 1411 ms P50 and gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) at 95.1% / 808 ms. Neither model has a shutdown date, and 3.8 Flash's floor is thinking_level low - MINIMAL is a validation error on it. The same split exists on generateContent (google/llm) and the subclass (google/interactions-vertex-llm), so the three reports pose one question. — *needs a call: should the two Interactions classes agree on one default, and is 99.9% over 95.1% on the benchmark worth ~750 ms of TTFAT?*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-09.md) — temperature and top_p are forwarded to the Interactions API, which deprecates them and whose Gemini 3 backend ignores them — `_build_request` warns when top_k, frequency_penalty or presence_penalty is set (the Interactions API has no such field) but puts temperature and top_p into generation_config silently. google-genai marks both deprecated on the Interactions GenerationConfig ('This will be removed in a future release'), and Google's Gemini 3.8 Flash guide says the backend ignores them. A probe with temperature=0.3 and top_p=0.9 still returns 200, so this is silent today in both directions. — *needs a call: extend the existing _warn_unsupported path to temperature and top_p on Gemini 3 models, or only document the deprecation on the Settings fields?*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-09.md) — google-genai 3.0.0 is outside the `>=2.25.0,<3` pin — 3.0.0 shipped 2026-11-06; the installed 2.34.0 is the highest the pin allows. For this class the breaking change that bites is the Gemini Developer API default moving from v1beta to v1, since Interactions features ship on v1beta/v1alpha first (the video blocks in function results, added 2026-11-05, are v1beta and v1alpha only); 3.0.0 also makes CodeExecutionResultStep.result a union and httpx2 the default HTTP client. The pin is shared by every Google service. — *needs a call: when does the google extra move to `<4`, and does this class then need to pin api_version=v1beta to keep Interactions features that are not on v1?*
- [google/interactions-vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-vertex-llm/2026-11-09.md) — The gemini-3.8-flash default answers about 50% slower than gemini-3.6-flash on Vertex, and GoogleInteractionsLLMService defaults to 3.6 while this subclass defaults to 3.8 — Interleaved medians of 5 through this class, trivial prompt: gemini-3.8-flash TTFAT 1072 ms (spread 892-1165 ms) against 699 ms for gemini-3.6-flash (spread 576-895 ms), thinking_ms 3 for both, 5/5 runs ok for each. The quality case favours 3.8: aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard has gemini-3.8-flash (low) at 99.9% / 1411 ms P50 against gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) at 95.1% / 808 ms. Neither model has a shutdown date. The split is Vertex-vs-Gemini-API rather than Interactions-vs-generateContent: both Vertex classes ship 3.8, both Gemini API classes ship 3.6. — *needs a call: should the two Interactions classes agree on one default, or is the Vertex pair deliberately on 3.8 for the benchmark score while the Gemini API pair stays on 3.6 for latency?*
- [google/interactions-vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-vertex-llm/2026-11-09.md) — probe.py cannot construct GoogleInteractionsVertexLLMService, so this unit has no first-tier probe — `build_kwargs` follows `**kwargs` into base classes, finds GoogleInteractionsLLMService's required `api_key` and passes GOOGLE_API_KEY, which this subclass rejects with ValueError; with the variable unset it exits 2 instead. The registry entry's include_params (credentials, location, project_id) also map to GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS and GOOGLE_PROJECT_ID, which CI exports as GOOGLE_VERTEX_TEST_CREDENTIALS and GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT_ID. Every probe below ran through a scratch wrapper that drops the api_key kwarg and maps those two names. The same gap is open on google/vertex-llm (since 2026-10-28), with the same root cause. — *needs a call: should build_kwargs skip inherited parameters a subclass rejects, and should the Vertex credential variables CI exports be the names probe.py looks for?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-09.md) — gemini-3.8-flash (GA 2026-09-02) supersedes the gemini-3.6-flash default and now outscores it on aiewf-eval, but is still ~60% slower in probes (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — The quality half of the bump now passes — aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard has a gemini-3.8-flash (low) row at 99.9% pass against gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) at 95.1%. Latency still fails: TTFAT 1616 ms vs 1024 ms (median of 5, trivial prompt) and 1890 ms vs 960 ms (median of 3, river-crossing prompt), with thinking_ms 0, matching the leaderboard's own P50s of 1411 ms vs 808 ms. 3.8 Flash's floor is thinking_level low; it has no minimal level. — *needs a call: is a ~600 ms TTFAT regression worth 99.9% over 95.1% on the benchmark, or does the default stay on the faster model?*

- [google/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Hybrid VAD is unreachable; the service never sends `audio_stream_end` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — The capabilities guide documents hybrid VAD as the latency-preferred shape — leave server VAD on for speech start and as a fallback, and have the client's VAD send `audio_stream_end` at end of speech so the server finalizes without waiting out its silence timer. `audio_stream_end` is a field on `LiveSendRealtimeInputParameters` in google-genai 2.34.0 but appears nowhere in the service, which offers only server-VAD-only or fully manual activity_start/end turns (where the guide notes the server prepends no pre-speech audio). Re-probed with `extra={"timestamp_granularities":["word"]}`: the call transcribed normally rather than being rejected, which is how Groq answers that combination against the service's `response_format=json` — the key never left Pipecat. — *needs a call: a stance on whether `extra` should be spread into the transcription request for the whisper services generally, and whether word timestamps are useful before `TranscriptionFrame` carries them*

- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-09.md) — LiveAvatar LITE `error` and `warning` events are dropped silently (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — probed again today — a deliberately bad command came back as `{"type": "error", "error": {"type": "invalid_request_error", ...}}` and `_handle_ws_server_event` logged it at trace level as an unknown event. The events page now documents `error.type` as `invalid_request_error`, `server_error` or `video_starvation`, so video generation falling behind the audio Pipecat feeds it is an error rather than the warning it was in September; `warning` is left for non-fatal diagnostics such as a video frame gap. — *needs a call: whether these should become `push_error` (and at what fatality), given that `video_starvation` is expected under load and would be noisy as an ErrorFrame*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-09.md) — Client sends WebSocket commands without waiting for session.state_updated = connected (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — LiveAvatar's LITE events page still requires waiting for that state before any command event and says earlier commands are not accepted; `HeyGenClient._handle_ws_server_event` only logs it, so `agent.speak` audio queued in the first moments of a session can be sent too early — *needs a call: a call on whether to buffer or drop pre-connected audio, since gating changes session startup timing*
- [hume/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/hume/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `HumeAudioClassifier.min_audio_secs` is 5.0, but Hume lowered the Audio Decisions minimum to 1 second on 2026-11-07 — concerns `pipecat.classifiers.hume.audio_decisions`, not the TTS service; no inventory unit covers the classifier, so hume/tts is the only place this provider change surfaces. The property, two docstrings and the example comment all state the old 5-second floor, so short turns are still padded with the audio before them — *needs a call: whether a 1-second clip classifies well enough to stop padding, given the padding was a deliberate choice about what Hume hears*
- [inception/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inception/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Service sends no reasoning_effort default, so Inception's server-side "medium" applies to every turn (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — CONTRIBUTING's "Reasoning Defaults for LLM Services" asks each LLM service to send every model it lists the lowest reasoning setting the provider documents; Inception's service keeps no such table. For mercury-3 the documented floor is reasoning_effort=instant. Probed on mercury-3: 525 ms median TTFAT at the provider default vs 324 ms at instant on a reasoning-triggering prompt (337 ms trivial), so the cost is ~200 ms on hard turns, far less than the 1.6 s it was on mercury-2.5. Inception's own voice cookbook uses "low", and `examples/function-calling/function-calling-inception.py` already sets "low". — *needs a call: `instant` also drops mercury-3's default token budget from 24,576 to 8,192, and `LLMService._apply_thinking_default()` merges dict-valued request fields while Inception's `reasoning_effort` is a top-level string — a maintainer has to pick the value and the shape before this is mechanical*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Inworld's `response.speak` client event (speak exact text through the session TTS, no LLM) has no route from Pipecat — shipped 2026-09-23; a probe sent `response.speak` and got `response.speak.acknowledged accepted=true` followed by a full audio response. It carries `force_uninterruptible` and `metadata`, and maps onto `TTSSpeakFrame`, which this service does not handle — no realtime service in the tree does — *needs a call: whether a realtime LLM service should consume `TTSSpeakFrame`, and how `force_uninterruptible` relates to Pipecat's own `interruptible` flag*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Inworld accepts `input_image` content parts on `conversation.item.create`, but `ItemContent.type` has no image member and the service handles no image frames (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — confirmed on the wire last run (a data-URL `input_image` part completed a response); `events.py` still has no `image_url`/`detail` fields and `llm.py` mentions images nowhere — *needs a call: whether a realtime service should consume `InputImageRawFrame` directly or take images only from the `LLMContext`, and whether an image append should trigger a response*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — LLM generation controls have no route to Inworld: `max_output_tokens`, `tool_choice`, `truncation` and `text_generation_config` (reasoning effort, topP, penalties) (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `LLMSettings` already carries `max_tokens`, `top_p`, penalties and `seed`, and the service logs them as unhandled; Inworld names them differently and nests generation params in camelCase under `text_generation_config`. Reasoning is the sharp edge: `reasoning.effort` defaults to `MEDIUM` once a `reasoning` object is present, and the leaderboard row that makes `gpt-6-luna` attractive was run at `low` — *needs a call: whether to map the inherited `LLMSettings` fields onto Inworld's names or expose `text_generation_config` verbatim as a nested settings model*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `LLMMessagesAppendFrame` is unsupported — the handler only logs a warning (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Inworld accepts `conversation.item.create` for user, assistant and system messages, so appending is expressible; `conversation.item.retrieve`, `conversation.item.delete` and `conversation.item.truncate` are unused too — *needs a call: a decision on how appended messages interact with the server-owned conversation state (whether an append should also trigger a response)*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Inworld's Reasoning (`synthesisContext.reasoning` / `reasoningContext`) is not reachable from Settings — shipped 2026-10-28 for `inworld-tts-2`; a model writes a steering instruction for each line before synthesis, and `reasoningContext` (max 600 chars) carries the scene or character — *needs a call: a call on whether a TTS service should opt into a pre-synthesis model step at all, since Inworld says it "adds a short step before synthesis starts"*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `synthesisContext.previousRequests` is not sent, so each sentence is synthesized without the turn's earlier text (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Inworld says it "can improve the quality of the generation, especially for short or ambiguous input text" — exactly the sentence-at-a-time traffic a Pipecat pipeline sends; now documented on the WebSocket create config too, not only the HTTP endpoints — *needs a call: a call on where the previous-request history lives and how far back it goes (the cap is 5 entries / 2,000 characters) — this is service state, not a pass-through field*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `applyTextNormalization` is unreachable from `InworldHttpTTSService`, though the HTTP endpoints document it and the WebSocket class exposes it (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `InworldTTSService` takes it as an init-only `apply_text_normalization` argument and sends it in the create-context config; the HTTP class has neither the argument nor the payload key — *needs a call: whether to match the WebSocket class with a constructor argument or move both onto the shared `InworldTTSSettings` — adding only a Settings field would give the WebSocket class two ways to set the same thing*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Workspace pronunciation dictionaries (`enableCustomPronunciation`, `pronunciationDictionarySettings`) are not reachable — both endpoints document them for `inworld-tts-2` with a `language` set; Pipecat's own pronunciation path is inline IPA via `format_pronunciation`, which still works — *needs a call: a shape for a nested list of workspace resource names, plus a stance on the documented mutual exclusion between the two fields*
- [keenable/tool](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/keenable/tool/2026-11-09.md) — An operator cannot pin the voice-relevant search knobs; the constructor takes only `mode` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `max_results` (1-50, default 10), `snippet_max_length` (180-10000) and now `disable_cards` (Nov 2026) are all in the live `search_web_pages` schema, so today only the model sets them; an unbounded search returned 21 951 characters last run against 997 for the same query pinned to `max_results=2, disable_cards=true`, and that difference lands in the LLM context on every search turn — *needs a call: whether to grow the constructor past `mode` — named arguments versus a pinned-arguments passthrough to `MCPClient(tools_arguments=...)`*
- [kokoro/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/kokoro/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Model downloads are pinned to the model-files-v1.0 release, superseded by model-files-v1.1 (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — kokoro-onnx 0.6.x and the upstream README download kokoro-v1.0.onnx and voices-v1.0.bin from model-files-v1.1; those re-exports add a duration output (create_timed timestamps, continuous synthesis) and embed the vocab config. 0.6.1 still loads the old export, so nothing is broken today. — *needs a call: the v1.1 files carry the same names as the cached v1.0 ones, so bumping the URL alone leaves existing installs on the old graph; a cache-key change is a decision.*
- [mem0/memory](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mem0/memory/2026-11-09.md) — Mem0 search options `rerank`, `categories` and metadata filters are not reachable from InputParams (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — mem0 2.0.0 flipped the `rerank` default from true to false, so recall is plain hybrid ranking; both `MemoryClient.search` and OSS `Memory.search` take `rerank`, and a `rerank: bool = False` field would pass straight through. The wider set (`latest_only`, `keyword_search`, `reference_date`) is unchanged in 2.2.1; `reference_date` is still cloud-only — OSS `Memory.search` raises on it — *needs a call: reranking adds 150-200 ms per turn on the memory-retrieval path, which sits in front of the LLM call; someone has to say whether that is opt-in only or should become Pipecat's default, and whether cloud-only options belong in a shared InputParams*
- [mem0/memory](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mem0/memory/2026-11-09.md) — Mem0 add options (`custom_instructions`, `infer`, `expiration_date`, caller metadata) are not reachable (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_store_messages` sends only `messages` plus a fixed `metadata={"platform": "pipecat"}`; AddMemoryOptions in mem0ai 2.2.1 also accepts custom_instructions, agent_custom_instructions, infer, expiration_date, timestamp, custom_categories, structured_data_schema — *needs a call: whether caller metadata merges with or replaces the `platform` tag, and which of the add options are worth surfacing rather than exposing the whole options object*
- [mem0/memory](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mem0/memory/2026-11-09.md) — Local-config path ANDs multiple entity IDs where the cloud path ORs them (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — with `local_config` and more than one of user_id/agent_id/run_id set, `_retrieve_memories`/`get_memories` pass flat keys, which mem0 ANDs; ANDing user_id and agent_id matches only records carrying both, which the default extraction never writes, so recall silently returns nothing. The cloud path builds `{"OR": [...]}` and behaves as intended — *needs a call: OSS `Memory.search` still raises unless `filters` carries user_id, agent_id or run_id as a flat top-level key, so a bare OR filter is rejected; matching the cloud OR semantics needs a decision on the local filter shape (and ideally one local-client run to confirm)*
- [meta/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/meta/stt/2026-11-09.md) — DIARIZATION finals may be pushed twice, once from `transcript.final` and once from `speechComplete` (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — Meta's schema page says `final: true` marks completion in PUSH_TO_TALK, and the speech-to-text page's per-mode table gives `speechComplete` as the final for both ENDPOINTING and DIARIZATION; `_handle_transcript` finalizes whenever `mode != "ENDPOINTING"`, so a diarized turn carrying a final-flagged transcript would emit two TranscriptionFrames. Fix is the condition `mode == "PUSH_TO_TALK"`. — *needs a call: needs a live DIARIZATION session to confirm the server emits final-flagged transcripts in that mode; META_API_KEY is unset so nothing could be probed*
- [meta/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/meta/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Diarization speaker labels are dropped instead of reaching the pipeline (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `speaker` events (`label` `A`, `B`, ...) are trace-logged only and `speechComplete` carries no label, so DIARIZATION output reaches the pipeline unattributed. Meta's own batch endpoint renders the same data as `A:Hello` / `unknown:<transcript>` in its `text/plain` response, which is a third formatting precedent alongside Pipecat's. — *needs a call: a call on how to surface it — Speechmatics formats the speaker into the transcript text, Soniox annotates tokens, Meta's own text/plain uses a `label:` prefix*
- [minimax/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/minimax/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `stream_options.exclude_aggregated_audio` is never sent, so every stream re-downloads the complete audio in its final chunk (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the deprecated `InputParams.exclude_aggregated_audio` documents the option but nothing reads it; `run_tts` then discards the aggregated chunk by skipping blocks carrying `extra_info` — *needs a call: `stream_options` is only valid when `stream=True`, so exposing it means deciding whether to gate on `self._stream` or simply always exclude the aggregate*
- [minimax/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/minimax/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `voice_modify` (pitch, intensity, timbre, sound_effects) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — a T2A addition from 2025-07-19; nested object with three -100..100 scales plus a sound_effects enum — *needs a call: whether to model it as a nested settings dataclass or a pass-through dict, and whether its pitch overlaps confusingly with the existing top-level pitch*
- [minimax/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/minimax/tts/2026-11-09.md) — MiniMax's WebSocket T2A endpoint has no Pipecat service (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — wss://api.minimax.io/ws/v1/t2a_v2 keeps one connection across turns and takes incremental text via task_continue; the HTTP class pays a fresh POST per utterance (median TTFB 383 ms measured) — *needs a call: a new service class is out of scope for an automatic PR; needs a maintainer to decide whether MiniMax warrants a WebSocket implementation*
- [mistral/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Mistral Small 4's reasoning mode is unreachable — `reasoning_effort` is not a Settings field, and setting it via `extra` yields list-valued `LLMTextFrame.text` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — reproduced again this run. Mistral's new `prompt_mode="reasoning"` is worse off: the OpenAI client rejects it outright as an unknown keyword argument, so it is reachable only as `extra={"extra_body": {...}}`. Magistral models stream plain strings and are unaffected. — *needs a call: exposing the field is the easy half; the content-part shape has to be flattened in the OpenAI base path, which touches every OpenAI-compatible service — someone has to decide where that handling lives.*
- [mistral/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `service_tier` is accepted by the inherited constructor but never sent — `MistralLLMService.build_chat_completion_params` omits it (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Mistral's API reference documents `service_tier` with values `auto` and `standard_only`; our override drops the value silently. — *needs a call: Mistral's enum (`auto`/`standard_only`) differs from the OpenAI values the shared constructor argument documents, so wiring it through needs a call on whether to reuse the argument or give Mistral its own typed setting.*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `mistralai` pin `>=2.0.0,<3` blocks 3.x, where the realtime session gained `language`, `context_bias`, `diarize` and `speaker_references` — latest is 3.7.0 (2026-11-01); every symbol stt.py imports and the whole RealtimeConnection surface it uses (send_audio, flush_audio, is_closed, update_session) exist unchanged in 3.7.0, so the pin move looks mechanical for this service — *needs a call: a stance on bumping a provider SDK major, which also moves MistralTTSService and the lockfile, so it is not an STT-only call*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Realtime 3's built-in end-of-turn detection is not wired into Pipecat's turn strategies — SDK 3.x exposes a `turn_detection` session config (start_threshold, eager_end_threshold, end_threshold, min_silence_ms, end_timeout_ms) and `turn.start` / `turn.eager_end` / `turn.resume` / `turn.end` events; the service pushes neither, and today it flushes audio off VADUserStoppedSpeakingFrame instead — *needs a call: whether a provider-side turn signal should drive a user turn start/stop strategy, which is a design call beyond a Settings field — and it depends on the SDK pin moving first*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `language` in MistralSTTSettings is documented as a transcription hint but is never sent (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — no longer inherently unsupported — the realtime session takes `language` from mistralai 3.x on — but under our `<3` pin the value is still dropped and changing it only costs a reconnect via `_update_settings` — *needs a call: whether to pass it through once the SDK pin moves, or correct the docstring now so the field stops claiming an effect it has not got*

- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Voxtral 3 speech parameters (expressiveness, phoneme_hints, conversation_history) are not reachable from Settings — mistralai 3.7.0's `SpeechRequest` adds `expressiveness` (guidance scale >= 1.0), `phoneme_hints` (word -> IPA phones) and `conversation_history` (prior turns conditioning delivery, preset `pvc` voices only); the 2.10.1 request our pin allows has none of them — *needs a call: the SDK pin has to move to 3.x first, and `conversation_history` is restricted to preset pvc voices, so exposing it needs a stance on unsupported voices*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Mistral's one-off reference audio (`ref_audio`, zero-shot voice cloning) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the speech request takes `ref_audio` (base64 clip) as an alternative to `voice_id`; a Settings field passed into `complete_async` would cover it — *needs a call: `run_tts` calls `assert_given(self._settings.voice)`, so voice is mandatory today; exposing `ref_audio` needs a stance on making voice optional and on which wins when both are set*

- [moondream/vision](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moondream/vision/2026-11-09.md) — Moondream's `query` affordances (reasoning, sampling settings, streaming) are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — query(image, question, reasoning, spatial_refs, stream, settings) is unchanged at the pinned revision; MoondreamSettings adds no field beyond model, and run_vision calls query(image_embeds, text) positionally then yields one VisionTextFrame after the whole answer — *needs a call: whether reasoning and sampling belong in Settings, and whether streaming should change the emitted frame shape*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Keyterm biasing (`set_keyterms` / `set_context`) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — moonshine-voice 0.1.6 `Transcriber.set_keyterms(list)` and `set_context(text, max_terms)` bias decoding toward supplied terms; neither is called, and `keyterm_boost` (default 2.0) sets the strength at load time — *needs a call: both raise on non-streaming architectures (`tiny`, `base`), so exposing them needs a stance on what happens when the configured model cannot bias*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Alphanumeric spelling mode is never enabled although its model is downloaded (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `get_model_for_language` pre-fetches the language's `spelling_cnn.ort` best-effort, but `Transcriber` is built without `spelling_model_path` and `transcribe_without_streaming` runs with the default `flags=0`, so `MOONSHINE_FLAG_SPELLING_MODE` never applies — *needs a call: whether spelling fusion should be a Settings flag or on by default, and whether it costs latency on the CPU path*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Transcription is batch-only; Moonshine's streaming API and its partial results are unused (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the service is a `SegmentedSTTService` calling `transcribe_without_streaming` once per VAD segment; `Transcriber.add_audio` plus a listener emits interim text at `update_interval`, and 0.1.6 adds `create_stream`/`get_default_stream` for concurrent streams — *needs a call: a decision on whether Moonshine gets a second, streaming service class alongside the segmented one*
- [nebius/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nebius/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default Qwen/Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507 is unbenchmarked while Nebius hosts models with published aiewf-eval scores (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — aiewf-eval still has no medium-context row for the default; Nebius now serves meta-models/muse-spark-open, whose row is the best of any Nebius-hosted model (96.7%, 626 ms on Baseten at low reasoning), but through our class it is 625 ms against the default's 445 ms even at that setting — *needs a call: Nebius designates no successor to the default, so picking a new one is a Pipecat quality/cost call with no latency case behind it*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Neuphonic's word timings (`timestamps` / `alignment`) are not requested or surfaced (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — added to the TTS websocket in September 2026; `TTSService.add_word_timestamps` exists, but `NeuphonicTTSService` extends `InterruptibleTTSService`, which is explicitly the base for services without word timestamps and reconnects the socket on every interruption — *needs a call: adopting timings means moving the websocket class off `InterruptibleTTSService` onto word-timestamp interruption handling — a structural change, not a value*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The WebSocket service ignores Neuphonic's `stop`, `context_id` and `<RESET>` signals and never closes its audio context (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_receive_messages` reads only `data.audio`; every other WebSocket TTS service in Pipecat calls `remove_audio_context` when the provider signals end-of-generation, and `<RESET>` would drop buffered text on interruption instead of reconnecting — *needs a call: keying audio contexts off Neuphonic's `context_id`/`stop` changes interruption and turn behavior, not just a value — needs a maintainer's call on the shape*
- [nvidia/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `/no_think` in the NVIDIA examples' system prompt does not disable reasoning (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — re-probed this run against the new default with the examples' prompt verbatim: it still reasoned (479 ms of a 1034 ms TTFAT), while the documented switch `extra_body={"chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": false}}` answered in 328 ms with no thought frames — *needs a call: dropping the dead prefix or passing the documented switch is a choice, and aiewf-eval has no row at all for super-vl-120b to say what the switch costs in quality*
- [nvidia/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/llm/2026-11-09.md) — NIM's chat_template_kwargs reasoning controls have no Settings field (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `enable_thinking` is documented for the default super-vl-120b (super-120b also documented `low_effort` and `force_nonempty_content`) but reachable only as `extra={"extra_body": {"chat_template_kwargs": {...}}}`, since `Settings.extra` is spread as top-level OpenAI kwargs — *needs a call: a pass-through field would have to define what happens when a user also sets `extra_body` directly*
- [nvidia/omni-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/omni-llm/2026-11-09.md) — Omni reasons on every turn and the reasoning switch has no Settings field — reasoning is on by default on the model card; probed, a trivial prompt costs 472 ms of thinking before the first answer token (TTFAT 680 ms) and a harder one 1445 ms (TTFAT 1593 ms), while `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false` answers in 183 ms with no thought frames and `reasoning_budget=16` in 413 ms; reachable only as `extra={"extra_body": {"chat_template_kwargs": {...}}}`, and `examples/voice/voice-nvidia-omni.py` sets neither — *needs a call: a pass-through field has to define what happens when a user also sets `extra_body` directly (nvidia/llm tracks the same question for super-120b), and no benchmark says what capping Omni's reasoning costs in quality*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — NvidiaSTTService never applies the NVIDIA locale map, so base language codes reach the NIM unmapped (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `language_to_nvidia_nemotron_speech_language` is defined in `nvidia/stt.py` but only `NvidiaSegmentedSTTService` wires it into `language_to_service_language`; the streaming class inherits `STTService`'s base, which returns the enum unchanged, so `Language.PT` goes out as `pt` where the support matrix only names `pt-BR` and `pt-PT`. The hosted endpoint tolerates `language=en` (probed again this run), so the remaining risk is unmapped locales on self-hosted multilingual NIMs — *needs a call: sign-off on the behavior change — base codes would start resolving to locales (EN to en-US, PT to pt-BR, ES to es-ES) for deployments that rely on today's pass-through*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The locale map covers 13 of the 40 language-locales Nemotron ASR Streaming supports (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — mapped locales are ar-AR, de-DE, en-GB, en-US, es-ES, es-US, fr-FR, hi-IN, it-IT, ja-JP, ko-KR, pt-BR, ru-RU; six of the 19 transcription-ready locales (nl-NL, fr-CA, pt-PT, tr-TR, uk-UA, vi-VN) and both lower tiers fall back to a bare base code — *needs a call: verification requires a self-hosted multi-type NIM — the hosted NVCF deployment is en-US only, so added entries cannot be probed*
