- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Settings.model is never sent in the Riva RecognitionConfig, so self-hosted multi-model NIMs cannot be told which model to use (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — the streaming `_create_recognition_config` pins `model=""` and the segmented one omits the field; on the hosted NVCF path the function id selects the model, and the 2026-09-14 ad-hoc probe had the hosted Canary deployment answer INVALID_ARGUMENT "Model canary-1b-asr is not available on server" when the name is sent, so a plain pass-through would break hosted users — *needs a call: whether to send `Settings.model` only when the caller overrode it or when `server` is not the NVCF endpoint — either way it needs a rule for telling the two deployment shapes apart*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — NvidiaSegmentedSTTService silently ignores speaker_diarization and diarization_max_speakers (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — both fields are inherited from `_NvidiaBaseSTTSettings` into `NvidiaSegmentedSTTSettings`, but the segmented `_create_recognition_config` never calls `add_speaker_diarization_to_config`, so setting them is a no-op with no warning; the streaming class passes them through, including 26.10.0's eight-speaker Sortformer ceiling — *needs a call: whether hosted Canary supports offline diarization at all — if not, the fields belong on the streaming settings class rather than the shared base*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — A permanent gRPC INVALID_ARGUMENT is treated as a transient stream drop and retried forever (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_response_handler` turns every `grpc.RpcError` into a drop reason and `_handle_stream_drop` answers it with `_request_reconnect()`; the `language=de-DE` probe below again produced no transcript and no ErrorFrame in 30 s — *needs a call: which gRPC status codes should be fatal rather than retried, and whether that belongs in the NVIDIA service or in STTService's reconnect policy*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — NvidiaSageMakerSTTSettings exposes none of the NIM realtime session options (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — the class adds no fields of its own, and `_send_session_config` hard-codes `recognition_config.enable_automatic_punctuation: true` and sends nothing else; NIM's realtime `transcription_session.update` also takes `max_alternatives`, `enable_word_time_offsets`, `enable_profanity_filter`, `enable_verbatim_transcripts`, `speaker_diarization`, `word_boosting` and `endpointing_config`, all of which the gRPC classes already expose — *needs a call: a deployed SageMaker endpoint to probe the pass-through against — no NVIDIA SageMaker endpoint name is configured in this environment*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The sagemaker extra pins aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2 below 0.10, three releases behind PyPI (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — `pyproject.toml` still has `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2>=0.6.0,<0.10`; 0.10.0 (2026-08-20), 0.11.0 (2026-08-24) and 0.12.0 (2026-10-08) are all excluded, and no 0.13 shipped since the last run. The package calls itself experimental with breaking changes expected before 1.0, so the pin is defensible, but it has not moved since August — *needs a call: whether the bidi-stream client in `services/aws/sagemaker/bidi_client.py` still works on 0.12 — no SageMaker endpoint here to run it against*
- [nvidia/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Magpie's word timestamps (`enable_word_time_offsets`) are neither requested nor consumed (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — riva-client 2.28.0 still returns per-word start/end times in `SynthesizeSpeechResponse.meta.words` for Magpie Multilingual and Zeroshot; `_build_base_request` never sets the flag and both response loops read only `item.audio` — *needs a call: a call on wiring Riva's streaming response metadata into TTSService's word-timestamp API (start/reset/add), which is more than a request-field pass-through*
- [ollama/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ollama/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default model llama2 predates tool calling and is not what Ollama tells users to run (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — Still reproduces on 8a4cb21cc: `__init__` hardcodes `self.Settings(model="llama2")` and a construction check prints `default model: llama2`. llama2 remains pullable (library page still "Updated 2 years ago", text-only tags, no `tools` tag, no deprecation notice) but has slipped to #25 by pulls (7.6M), so `OLLamaLLMService()` with no settings cannot do function calling. Every in-tree caller overrides it (examples use `llama3.2` and `llama3.1:8b`, the eval judge and CLI templates `gemma4:12b`). aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard grew to 50 rows and still has no llama row and no Ollama-served row, so no candidate can be ranked the way an LLM default bump requires. — *needs a call: Ollama designates no successor and the default only matters for a model the user has already pulled — does a local-runtime service want an opinionated default at all, or should the model become required?*
- [openai/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/image/2026-11-09.md) — Default gpt-image-2.5-flare predates gpt-image-2.6-flare (released 2026-11-04) — 2.6 is what the deprecations page now recommends for the models retiring on 2026-12-01, and 2.6-flare generated faster than 2.5-flare in the probe; OpenAI has not deprecated 2.5-flare and still calls it current, and `quality` values `xhigh`/`max` are documented for the 2.5 models only — *needs a call: a call on whether to follow OpenAI's replacement recommendation while 2.5-flare is still current, given that a bump would reject the `xhigh`/`max` quality tiers users can set today*
- [openai/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/image/2026-11-09.md) — Images API request parameters background, output_format, output_compression, n and stream/partial_images are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — none is a bare pass-through - `background=transparent` constrains `output_format`, `output_compression` is only valid with webp/jpeg, and `n`/`stream` change what `run_image_gen` yields (it sends `n=1` and reads `data[0]`); 2.6 adds the same shape again with `output_format=svg|gif` plus `svg`, `frame_count` and `frame_duration_ms`, which no longer decode into a `URLImageRawFrame` — *needs a call: a call on whether image generation should yield more than one frame per prompt and whether non-raster output (SVG, animated GIF) is in scope at all*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Outbound SIP calling cannot be started or attached to — an outbound call is placed with `POST /v1/live/sessions` carrying `transport.type: "sip"` plus `transport.destination` and `transport.trunk`, and the app then attaches to `wss://api.openai.com/v1/live/sessions/{id}/attach` **without** sending `session.start`. The service only ever dials `base_url` over WebSocket and sends `session.start` from `_send_session_config`, so neither half is reachable; `transport.ringing` / `transport.answered` / `transport.failed` have no models in events.py either. The endpoint is live on this account: a private-IP trunk is refused by parameter name (`transport.trunk.provider_url`), so the schema is recognised. In a SIP session the audio never touches the pipeline, leaving Pipecat as the control plane (client-delegation backend, tools, transcripts) — *needs a call: whether Pipecat wants a mode where OpenAI owns the call audio and the pipeline carries no audio frames, given it needs session creation over REST, an attach-without-start path and transport events — and whether that same attach path should also serve forking*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Prompt-generated custom voices cannot be selected (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — a custom voice is selected by passing an object, `audio.output.voice = {"id": "voice_123"}`, but `OpenAILiveLLMSettings.voice` and `events.AudioOutputConfig.voice` are both typed `str | None`, so the only reachable voices are the named built-ins. Re-confirmed this run: the object form is validated as a voice reference ("No voice found with id"), while the same id as a plain string is refused as an unknown built-in ("Voice session access denied"), so widening the type is the only way in — *needs a call: whether `voice` widens to `str | <typed ref>` or `str | dict`, and whether the docs' advice to repeat the voice description in `instructions` should be reflected in the service*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Decisions transcript triggers are not expressible (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `session.triggers` runs a Decisions question over every `session.input_transcript.delta` or `session.output_transcript.delta` and returns the answers as `decision.event`, which is how an avatar picks an expression or gesture while the conversation runs. `SessionConfig` has no `triggers` field and events.py has no `decision.event` model, so a session started this way is accepted (the server echoed `triggers` back) but nothing in the pipeline would see the answers — *needs a call: where trigger answers should surface — a new frame, an event handler, or the thinking channel — since the config half is useless without a route for the results*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Pipecat's reasoning defaults are not applied to the delegated backend model (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `_responses_delegation_config` sends `reasoning` only when the caller configured it, while `OpenAIResponsesLLMService` fills in the model's `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` entry (the lowest documented effort) when it is unset. A delegation to `gpt-6-sol` therefore thinks at the server's default effort where the same settings object through the Responses service would send `effort: none` — *needs a call: whether the delegated path should adopt the reasoning-defaults table, which changes latency and cost for existing Responses-delegation bots that never set reasoning*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Developer and system messages the app appends mid-session never reach the API (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `_handle_context` deliberately forwards nothing from a later context, because user and assistant messages cannot be told apart from the service's own transcript recordings. A newly appended developer or system message can only have come from the app, and `instructions` stays appendable for the life of the session via `session.instructions.append` — the event class already exists in events.py and the service never sends it — *needs a call: whether the service should diff each incoming context for new developer/system messages, and whether they belong in instructions or in the thinking channel*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-09.md) — No first-class reasoning-effort setting on the chat-completions service (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — effort is the deciding latency lever on the recommended lineup and the untyped `extra` dict is the only way to reach it: on a reasoning-triggering prompt gpt-6.1-terra goes from 3173 ms to 808 ms median TTFAT with `extra={"reasoning_effort": "none"}`. The sibling Responses service has a typed `reasoning` field. — *needs a call: where the field would live — `OpenAILLMService.Settings` is `BaseOpenAILLMService.Settings`, shared by every OpenAI-compatible service, and the accepted values differ per model (gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol have no `none`) and per provider*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `pipecat eval`'s OpenAI default, DEFAULT_OPENAI_MODEL = "gpt-4o", shuts down 2027-01-14 — src/pipecat/evals/services.py:349 — the model a scenario gets when its judge or persona block says `service: openai` with no model. OpenAI names gpt-6-luna as the gpt-4o replacement, and gpt-6-luna drives LLMClassifier fine, but the constant decides eval verdicts. — *needs a call: which model should judge and play personas once gpt-4o is gone — the named gpt-6-luna, or a stronger tier, given the verdicts depend on it*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Four server-event fields the GA spec marks optional are still required, so a lawful payload kills the receive loop (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — Re-checked against openai 3.46.0, unchanged from 3.40.0: `ConversationItemInputAudioTranscriptionDelta` requires `content_index` and `delta` (GA: both optional, plus an unmodeled `logprobs`); `ConversationItemInputAudioTranscriptionFailed.error` is our `RealtimeError`, which requires `type` and `message` (GA gives that event its own Error with all four fields optional — a partial failure report would kill the session it is reporting on); `ItemContent.type` is a required Literal, but `response.content_part.added`/`.done` mark `part.type` optional; `RealtimeConversation` requires `id` and `object`, while GA's `conversation.created` marks both optional. Their models are shared with client events and the `error` event, which is why PR 6329 deferred them — *needs a call: whether the shared models get split (server-side copies with optional fields) or the receive loop stops raising on validation failure at all, which would subsume this and the MCP item-type gap*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — GPT-Live 1 is GA but unreachable — it is a separate API, not a Realtime model (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — GA 2026-09-10 at $0.05/min; full-duplex, reasoning delegated to a backend Responses model or the client. It lives on `v1/live/sessions` with its own protocol (`openai.types.live`); probed again this run through OpenAIRealtimeLLMService and rejected with `invalid_model`. It is where OpenAI's audio work keeps going — outbound SIP calling opened to all organizations on 2026-11-02, after native Decisions delegation (2026-10-15) and prompt-to-voice custom voices (2026-10-16) — while Realtime has had no changelog entry since 2026-07-28. The Realtime guide carries no deprecation banner and still builds its sessions with gpt-realtime-2.1 — *needs a call: whether GPT-Live gets its own Pipecat service (new unit, new protocol, a delegation model) or waits — too large for a provider-watch PR either way*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — server_vad turn detection has no idle_timeout_ms, create_response or interrupt_response (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — GA's ServerVad carries all three; our TurnDetection carries only threshold/prefix_padding_ms/silence_duration_ms, while SemanticTurnDetection already has create_response and interrupt_response. OpenAI documents idle_timeout_ms as the way to prompt a silent caller on a phone call, and since PR 6329 the `input_audio_buffer.timeout_triggered` it raises is skipped rather than fatal — *needs a call: under server VAD the service never sends response.create itself, so create_response=false would silence the bot permanently — does the pass-through ship with the hazard documented, or with service-side handling?*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — parallel_tool_calls is not reachable from SessionProperties (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — One of GA's 14 session fields and one of the two we do not model (truncation is PR 5547's). OpenAI documents it as "Only supported by reasoning Realtime models such as gpt-realtime-2"; probed in an earlier run, gpt-realtime-2.1 accepts it and gpt-realtime-1.5 fails the session with `invalid_value`, the same hazard `_strip_unsupported_reasoning` already absorbs for `reasoning` — *needs a call: whether the model-capability stripping generalizes to a second field, or parallel_tool_calls stays out until it does*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-09.md) — The reasoning default for gpt-6-luna is effort="none", the configuration aiewf-eval could not complete a conversation with (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` holds each model's lowest documented effort, so gpt-6-luna gets "none"; the aiewf-eval medium-context run of gpt-6-luna at "none" completed 0 of 30 multi-turn conversations while the same model at "low" scores 91.8%. A user who selects gpt-6-luna — the model OpenAI recommends in place of gpt-4.1 — gets "none" unless they set `reasoning` themselves — *needs a call: whether a model's documented lowest effort should give way to a working one when the lowest degrades multi-turn behavior, or whether a docstring note is enough*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-09.md) — OpenAI's mid-turn and long-running-work controls (async tool calling, mid-turn steering, configuration_update) are not supported (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — async tools set `async: true` on the tool definition and return results tagged INTERRUPT / WHEN_IDLE / SILENT, which would let a voice bot keep talking through a slow tool call; mid-turn steering is a WebSocket message the OpenAIResponsesLLMService transport could carry; the `configuration_update` input item changes reasoning.effort mid-conversation on the gpt-6 family. None is a pass-through parameter — each needs adapter and function-call-result work — *needs a call: whether any of the three is worth designing for now, and which one voice users need first*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — OpenAIRealtimeSTTService still defaults to gpt-realtime-whisper, which OpenAI's realtime transcription guide no longer lists (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the docs case is unchanged - gpt-live-transcribe is still the guide's recommended starting point, gpt-realtime-whisper is still absent from the guide and still absent from the deprecation table. The latency case has moved again, this time to parity: on clean ad-hoc sessions (audio held until session.updated, commit to final transcript, median of 3) gpt-realtime-whisper is 701 ms and gpt-live-transcribe 698 ms, where 2026-10-28 measured 692 vs 1014 ms. Latency no longer argues against the bump. It cannot be verified through the class while probe.py run fails 0/5 for both models, and the two models expose different Settings: delay is gpt-realtime-whisper only, keywords and languages are gpt-live-transcribe and gpt-transcribe only, and gpt-live-transcribe supports no server-side turn detection — *needs a call: whether to bump once the commit-empty teardown is fixed and the bump can be probed through the class, given that it also swaps which Settings fields the default model accepts*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The languages list, and the realtime session's keywords hints, have no Settings field (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — unchanged and re-confirmed against the current guides and the 3.46.0 SDK schema (realtime transcription accepts delay, keywords, language, languages, model, prompt). Both guides say languages replaces the singular language and that the two must not be sent together, while both services always send language, so the field has to suppress language rather than pass through. Realtime keywords is documented for gpt-transcribe and gpt-live-transcribe but not for the default gpt-realtime-whisper, so it needs the per-model omission _omit_unsupported_prompt already does for prompt — *needs a call: whether setting languages should silently drop the language the services always send, and whether realtime keywords should be silently dropped for models that reject it*
- [openai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Custom voice IDs are rejected by the VALID_VOICES allowlist (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the SDK's `SpeechCreateParams.Voice` is `Union[str, Literal[...13 built-ins...], VoiceID]` where `VoiceID` is `{"id": "voice_1234"}`; `run_tts` yields an ErrorFrame for anything outside the 13 names, so the request never leaves the process. Prompt-created voices remain Live-only; only audio-sample custom voices work on /v1/audio/speech — *needs a call: whether to let unknown voice strings (and the custom voice object shape) through and give up the client-side typo check; it cannot be probed end to end without an account that has a custom voice*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-09.md) — OpenRouter's normalized `reasoning` field is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `reasoning={enabled, effort, max_tokens, exclude}` with `effort: "none"` is how OpenRouter turns thinking off uniformly across the catalogue; it now also carries `context` and `mode` for GPT-5.6 and newer, and `GET /models` reports per-model `supported_efforts`, `default_effort` and `mandatory`. All of it reaches the API today only through `extra={"extra_body": {...}}`. The successor OpenAI names for our default reasons by default, so the off switch bears on the default-model decision — *needs a call: OpenAI-family models also take `reasoning_effort` through `extra`, and OpenRouter now accepts it as a top-level shorthand for `reasoning.effort`, so a first-class field has to say which wins*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Model-level fallback (the `models` array) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — OpenRouter retries the next model in `models` when the first errors; a voice bot would use it to survive a model outage mid-call. The companion `route` field stays documented as deprecated in favour of `provider.sort.partition`, so the gap is the array alone — *needs a call: a stance on how a fallback list interacts with `Settings.model` and with runtime model updates*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `service_tier` reaches the request only through the deprecated InputParams path (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `BaseOpenAILLMService` sends `service_tier` from the constructor argument it stores in `self._service_tier`, and `OpenAILLMSettings` still has no counterpart, so the field is neither runtime-updatable nor reachable once InputParams goes in 2.0.0. OpenRouter extends the tier vocabulary beyond OpenAI's set — *needs a call: the field lives in the OpenAI base shared by every compatible service, so promoting it to Settings is a base-class change rather than an OpenRouter one*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Service sends the deprecated `preset` field; Perplexity renamed it to `effort` — Perplexity renamed the Agent API's `preset` to `effort` in October 2026 — "a naming change only", with `preset` kept as a deprecated alias and no removal date. The API reference's request schema now lists `effort` and no longer lists `preset`. Pipecat writes `preset` in two places: the `PerplexityAgentLLMSettings.preset` field, forwarded into `extra_body` by `_build_response_params`, and `_THINKING_DEFAULT`, which every default request sends. Probed today: `effort` is accepted and really validated — a bogus value is rejected with HTTP 400 "the effort name or version does not exist" — so the wire change works. The docstring's list of levels is also one short: `ultra` was added 2026-11-05, after `ultra-preview` in October. — *needs a call: whether to rename the public `preset` Settings field to `effort` with a deprecation, or only switch the wire field now and leave the field named after the alias — the alias has no announced removal date, so nothing forces the breaking half yet*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` is the `fast` effort's fallback model, not its model — Perplexity's `fast` effort — the exact configuration the service sends when nothing is configured — runs `openai/gpt-6-luna`, naming `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` only as the fallback when it is unavailable (`fast@2026-10-16.0`). Latency favours the newer model: median TTFAT 1098 ms against 1205 ms over `--repeat 5`, and 1050 ms against 1364 ms on a reasoning-triggering prompt. aiewf-eval blocks the straight bump: at reasoning off, which is what `fast` sets, `gpt-6-luna (none)` is unranked on `aiwf_medium_context` — 0 of 30 conversations completed, fixed-900 pass 25.0% — against `gpt-5.6-luna (none)` at 84.4% / 708 ms P50. The pairing that does beat today's default is `gpt-6-luna (low)` at 91.8% / 917 ms P50. — *needs a call: whether to bump the model together with raising the service's reasoning default off `fast`, since gpt-6-luna only beats the current default at `low` reasoning and that trades roughly 200 ms of leaderboard TTFAT for the pass rate*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-09.md) — The `web_search` tool Pipecat attaches is not configurable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `PerplexityAgentLLMSettings.web_search` is a bool, and `_build_response_params` appends a bare `{"type": "web_search"}`. The tool documents `search_type` (`web` or `fast`), `max_results`, `max_tokens`, `max_tokens_per_page`, a `filters` object (domain, recency, published-before/after, last-updated-before/after) and `user_location`, none reachable by name. `search_type` is the one that bites: Fast Search moved `low`, `medium`, `high` and `xhigh` from $2.50 to $1.00 per 1,000 invocations, and the changelog does not list `fast` among them, so the default voice configuration may be on the slower, dearer search path. There is an escape hatch — `extra={"tools": [...]}` is merged before the append, and a `web_search` entry already there is left alone — so this is ergonomics, not a wall. — *needs a call: whether `web_search` should grow into a bool-or-dict (or gain a sibling field) and, before that, whether the `fast` effort already applies Fast Search — if it does, the default costs nothing extra and this drops to low*
- [piper/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/piper/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The piper extra omits piper-tts's zh, ja and th extras, so Chinese, Japanese and Thai voices cannot be phonemized (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — voices.json ships zh_CN, ja_JP and th_TH voices; piper.phonemize_chinese fails at import (unicode_rbnf missing) and phonemize_japanese / phonemize_thai import pyopenjtalk / tltk lazily, so those voices fail at synthesis time — *needs a call: the zh extra pulls transformers and g2pW, ja pulls pyopenjtalk-plus and th pulls tltk and pandas — a call on whether pipecat-ai[piper] carries them or the docs tell users to install them*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Our pin `pocket-tts>=2.1.0,<3` excludes pocket-tts 3.x and 4.x (4.0.1, 2026-10-21) (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — every call site we use (`TTSModel.load_model`, `get_state_for_audio_prompt`, `generate_audio_stream`, `sample_rate`) has the same signature in 4.0.1, and bare predefined voice names such as `alba` still resolve, now per language; behind the pin sit 15 extra configs, retrained French/Italian/Dutch/German/Spanish/Portuguese weights, a `stop: threading.Event` on `generate_audio_stream` that would let an interruption end decoding, `sampler_decode_steps` (replacing the deprecated `lsd_decode_steps`), a `checkpoint` argument, and a 5 ms chunk fade-in that removes the onset click — *needs a call: 3.x/4.x replace `load_model`'s hard `temp=0.7` default with a per-config `default_temperature` of 0.3 and the `english` alias now points at `english_2026-10`, so the bump changes how every language sounds for callers that never set `temp` — needs a call on whether that is acceptable*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-09.md) — French is mapped to the 24-layer model and Dutch is unmapped (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — pocket-tts 3.2.0 added a distilled 6-layer `french` and 3.3.0 added `dutch`, both retrained in 4.0.0; the code comment stating French has no distilled model is still accurate for the 2.1.0 the pin resolves to, and `language_to_pocket_tts_language` has no Dutch entry — *needs a call: both configs exist only in 3.x/4.x, so the map change cannot land before the pin bump above — needs that decision first*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-09.md) — No way to select a pocket-tts model variant or a custom config (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `PocketTTSSettings` inherits `model` but the service ignores it, so `english_2026-01`, the 24-layer variants, 4.0's six sub-20M English models (18M, 7M and 1.9M, each with a `refl` variant) and the 14 community configs the README lists are unreachable; `load_model` takes either `language` or `config` (local path, `https://` or `hf://`) — *needs a call: `load_model` rejects `language` and `config` together and weights load once at construction — needs a call on whether `model` names a pocket-tts config, or a separate `config` constructor argument does*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-09.md) — Default qwen-plus trails Alibaba's current generation (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — unchanged this run. The alias is still "functionally equivalent to qwen-plus-2025-12-01", carries no deprecation notice, keeps thinking off by default (deep-thinking page lists qwen-plus and qwen-flash as the thinking-off hybrids) and probes healthy at 946 ms TTFAT. Alibaba has shipped no new text model since qwen3.8-max-0902 (2026-09-02); September/October releases are omni, audio and image. aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard still carries no DashScope-hosted row, so parity for a candidate cannot be read off it — *needs a call: which successor to adopt, and whether a hosted model with no aiewf-eval row can replace a default that has no row either*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-09.md) — No first-class way to control Qwen's default-on thinking (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the deep-thinking page's default-on list has grown to the whole 3.5-3.8 plus/flash/max line plus qwen3.8-omni-flash, and `enable_thinking` / `thinking_budget` are still non-standard parameters the Python SDK can only pass through `extra_body`; only qwen3.8-omni-flash accepts OpenAI's `reasoning_effort`. Cost is visible on the dense tier too: qwen3.8-27b spent 577 ms thinking on a trivial prompt (TTFAT 1467 ms vs 946 ms for qwen-plus). Today the knob is `extra={"extra_body": {"enable_thinking": False}}`, which works but is undiscoverable — *needs a call: whether to add `enable_thinking`/`thinking_budget` Settings fields or follow the Anthropic precedent and disable thinking by default for thinking-capable Qwen models*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — A fixed ~1 s jitter buffer delays the first audio frame (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_jitter_buffer_bytes = 44100` withholds audio until ~1 s has accumulated at 22050 Hz; median TTFB is 534 ms over 5 runs this time (427-584 ms), in line with the 551-687 ms of the three previous runs and well above the ~200 ms time-to-first-sound earlier runs read off the WebSocket page — *needs a call: whether the buffer can shrink without playback gaps — the code comments cite 300-450 ms gaps between Resemble's audio bursts, which nobody has re-measured*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Coda's custom pronunciation (`customLexicon`) is unreachable from any Rime TTS class — Rime documents `customLexicon` (array of `{spelling, pronunciation}` in X-SAMPA, up to 500 entries, en/de/es/fr/it/pt) as Coda's pronunciation control; a probe POST with it returns 200 and WebSocket v1 accepts `custom_lexicon`. Coda ignores `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` without error, so `RimeTTSService.PRONOUNCE()` silently does nothing on the default model. — *needs a call: `RimeTTSSettings` is shared by the websocket and HTTP classes and the websocket class pastes settings into the URL query string unencoded, so a shared field needs a stance on how (or whether) the websocket class sends a JSON array*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Mist v3 rejects `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` for de/es/fr with HTTP 400, and the services send it regardless of language — Probed live: `lang=de` plus `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` returns 400 `invalid_input` ("not supported for language de"), `lang=en` returns audio. `RimeTTSService.PRONOUNCE()` sets the field implicitly, so a German or French Mist v3 bot loses the turn with no hint from the service. — *needs a call: a call on whether the services should drop or warn about model- and language-specific fields rather than pass the user's setting through*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-09.md) — RimeHttpTTSService pins a legacy HTTP path and takes no base-URL override (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `__init__` hard-codes `https://users.rime.ai/v1/rime-tts`; Rime's cheat sheet now documents per-model hosts (`https://coda.api.rime.ai/`, `https://mist.api.rime.ai/`) as where HTTP TTS must go, and no longer documents the pinned path or the old `users-east` regional hosts. Both hosts answered 200 to the payload the class builds. — *needs a call: exposing the host means a new constructor parameter (`base_url=`), and moving the default to the per-model hosts is a behavior change — both are public-API decisions*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Rime recommends `segment=never` for voice agents, but Pipecat flushes only at end of turn (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — under `segment=never` Rime synthesizes nothing until it receives a `flush`, and `TTSService` sends one only when the bot turn ends, so a user following Rime's guidance gets no audio until the whole turn is written. The services leave `segment` unset and rely on Rime's `bySentence` default. WebSocket v1 has no such trap — `RimeV1TTSService` maps token streaming onto `SPLIT_STRATEGY_ACCUMULATE_SENTENCE`. — *needs a call: following Rime's recommendation means flushing per aggregated utterance rather than per turn — a change in synthesis cadence, not a settings pass-through*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-09.md) — RimeV1TTSService never requests WebSocket v1 word timestamps and its docstring says v1 has none — the v1 `start` message carries `timestamps.enable`; a probe with it set came back with five aligned spans in `done`. Rime documents word timestamps for Coda v1 in de/en/es/fr/it/pt and Mist v3 in en, so a bot moving from `/ws3` to v1 loses the word timestamps the older class reports. — *needs a call: v1 delivers every span in the terminal `done` event, after the context's audio, so turning them on means deciding how late timestamps should feed word tracking*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-09.md) — provider-watch does not cover RimeV1TTSService — `inventory.py` keys unit type on the module stem, and `rime/tts_v1.py` is not `tts`, so RimeV1TTSService lands in no unit: no report covers it and `probe.py run --service RimeV1TTSService` fails with "unknown service class". It had to be probed by hand this run. — *needs a call: a call on whether `tts_v1` folds into the `rime/tts` unit or becomes its own variant unit*
- [sambanova/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sambanova/llm/2026-11-09.md) — SambaNova's non-OpenAI body parameters (`top_k`, `repetition_penalty`, `chat_template_kwargs`) are unreachable from Settings (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — unchanged in code — `build_chat_completion_params` never sends `top_k`, and `params.update(self._settings.extra)` feeds `chat.completions.create(**params)` as kwargs, so only `extra={"extra_body": {"top_k": 40}}` reaches the API; `do_sample` has dropped out of SambaNova's spec, `repetition_penalty` and `chat_template_kwargs` remain — *needs a call: `base_llm.py` documents that top_k is ignored for every openai-client service; making SambaNova the exception needs a stance on that and on how an `extra_body` we build merges with a user's own `extra["extra_body"]`*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — The allowlist blocks six models /v2 now serves, including two GA ones — Offered on /v2 chat completions and rejected by `_SUPPORTED_MODELS`: `glm5.3-flash` (GA 2026-10-22), `deepseekv4.1-flash-1017` (GA 2026-10-30), `deepseekv4.1-flash-1017-interactive` (over 200 tok/s against about 80 on the standard model), `deepseekv4-pro`, `kimi-k3.1-pro` and `qwen38-27b`. The last three are Provisioned Throughput only. `kimi-k3`, `mimo-v2.6-flash` and `mimo-v2.6-pro` are still in the catalogue but were withdrawn on 2026-11-06 and are absent from the chat enum, so they are not candidates. — *needs a call: each addition wants a probe to confirm the model answers through the class, and the account has no credits; the Provisioned Throughput models may not be reachable on a serverless key at all*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `reasoning_effort` accepts `medium` everywhere and forbids `none` and `max`, neither of which matches /v2 (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — `SarvamLLMSettings` types it `Literal["low", "medium", "high"]`. The /v2 reference now spells out the union as `none`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `max`, with a per-model subset: `low`/`high`/`max` on `glm5.3`, `glm5.3-flash` and `gemma4`; the full five on the DeepSeek models and `qwen38-27b`; `low`/`medium`/`high`/`max` on `kimi-k3.1-pro`, where `medium` runs as `high`. `medium` returned 400 on glm5.3 on 2026-10-12 and `max` worked while failing type checking. — *needs a call: whether to widen the Literal to the documented union and validate per model, or split the allowed values by model the way `_REASONING_MODELS` already splits support*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — sarvam-105b-conversations is far faster to first answer token and is the variant Sarvam positions for voice agents (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Median TTFAT 775 ms vs 4053 ms for sarvam-105b on 2026-10-12, both GA. The model page still calls sarvam-105b the flagship, so this is not a documented succession; the conversations variant has a 32K context window against 128K and is /v1-only. — *needs a call: aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard, re-read this run, still has no Sarvam rows, so the quality half of a default bump is unmeasured*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `_WIKI_GROUNDING_MODELS` names a parameter the docs no longer describe (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `wiki_grounding` appears nowhere in the /v2 chat reference or on any current model page, yet `_WIKI_GROUNDING_MODELS` holds `gemma4` and `sarvam-105b` and the service merges it into `extra_body` for them. `sarvam-105b-conversations` is in neither capability set although /v1 accepts both parameters, so users on it get an error frame. The `gemma4` half of this item is resolved: the /v2 reference now documents `reasoning_effort` as accepted on `gemma4` (`low`/`high`/`max`, no reasoning unless set), matching `_REASONING_MODELS`. — *needs a call: HTTP 200 does not prove a parameter takes effect, and tightening the sets turns silently-ignored parameters into error frames — needs a stance on validating against the docs or against what the API accepts*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-09.md) — `stream_options` is stripped from every request, so streaming token usage is unavailable (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — `build_chat_completion_params` pops `stream_options` unconditionally. The /v2 reference documents it as valid whenever `stream` is true and required to be top-level; `include_usage: true` adds a final chunk carrying usage including `reasoning_tokens` — the counts Pipecat's LLM metrics would report. — *needs a call: whether /v1, which serves sarvam-105b-conversations, also accepts `stream_options`; the pop is unconditional today and removing it blindly would change requests on both API versions*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-09.md) — CLI registry offers only SarvamSTTService, whose endpoint Sarvam labels legacy (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Sarvam's streaming-api page still calls that endpoint "the generally-available legacy WebSocket" and says realtime "supersedes this endpoint for new voice-agent and live-transcription work", with no sunset date; realtime is still described as beta there. `sarvam_stt` in `cli/registry/service_metadata.py` maps only to `SarvamSTTService`, and the unit now has three classes — *needs a call: whether SarvamRealtimeSTTService (or the new SarvamHttpSTTService) should join the registry's Sarvam STT entry while Sarvam still calls realtime beta*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-09.md) — bulbul:v3's `dict_id` (pronunciation dictionary) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — a `dict_id` field on `SarvamHttpTTSSettings` passed through in `run_tts` and `_send_config` would do it; still documented on both the REST body and the WebSocket config for v3 and v4-flash, and the REST endpoint read it when it was last probed (2026-10-28, a bogus id answering 404 "Pronunciation dictionary not found") — *needs a call: a successful probe needs a dictionary created through the Pronunciation Dictionary API, which mutates the account — decide whether Pipecat exposes the field without also managing dictionaries*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-09.md) — bulbul:v4-flash's pitch and loudness ranges are neither documented in the Settings nor enforced (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — the API caps pitch at ±0.5 and loudness at 0.1–2.5 for v3/v4 and rejected anything outside with a 400 and no audio when last probed, while `TTSModelConfig` carries a range for pace only and the deprecated `InputParams` still validates against the v2 ranges (pitch ±0.75, loudness 0.3–3.0); the Settings docstrings give no range. Sarvam's pages still disagree about whether v4-flash takes these fields at all — the REST and WebSocket references call them bulbul:v2-only, the v4-flash best-practice guide gives them ranges — and the 2026-10-28 probe had the live endpoint accepting both on v4-flash, so Pipecat's `supports_pitch`/`supports_loudness` are right as they stand — *needs a call: whether to add `pitch_range`/`loudness_range` to `TTSModelConfig` and clamp as pace does, or only correct the docstrings and the stale `InputParams` bounds*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Sarvam's cloned and saved voices are unreachable from either class (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — the Voice Cloning API synthesizes through its own endpoints — `POST /voices/clone`, `/voices/clone/stream` and a `GET /voices/clone/ws` that takes incremental text — not through `/text-to-speech`, so reaching it means a new class rather than a Settings field. Sarvam keeps building it out — `language_code` became optional on all three on 2026-10-26, and `remove_background_noise` was documented end to end on 2026-11-06 (needs sarvamai >= 0.1.37 for the cloning calls, >= 0.1.45 for the denoise fields; our pin is 0.1.28) — *needs a call: whether a saved `voice_id` also works as `speaker` on `/text-to-speech` (unverified — checking it means creating a voice on the account) and, if not, whether Pipecat wants a cloned-voice TTS class*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Sarvam retires bulbul:v3 on Voice Agents on 2026-11-10 while Pipecat defaults to it (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — the deadline is 2026-11-10, but the Text-to-Speech API is explicitly exempt — the models page still calls v3 "stable" and the API default when `model` is omitted, and the end-of-life notice still reads "Bulbul V3 stays available on the Text-to-Speech API". Sarvam calls v4-flash "recommended for new integrations", and v4-flash was the faster of the two through `SarvamTTSService` when last measured (TTFB 550 ms vs 646 ms, medians of 5, 2026-10-28). A bump is not a one-value change either way — v4-flash has no Malayalam or Odia voices and rejects every v3 short speaker name, so `voice="shubh"` configs and `ml-IN`/`od-IN` users break — *needs a call: whether Pipecat follows Sarvam's voice-agent guidance to v4-flash despite the TTS API keeping v3, and if so how the v3 speaker names and the two dropped languages are handled*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `output_audio_codec` and `output_audio_bitrate` are reachable only through the deprecated `InputParams` (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — on `SarvamTTSService` both are constructor-time `InputParams` fields with no `SarvamTTSSettings` equivalent, and `InputParams` is marked for removal in 2.0.0, so the WebSocket codec choice disappears with it; the REST class never sends a codec at all and always decodes WAV — *needs a call: the receive path builds `TTSAudioRawFrame` from the payload as PCM, so a non-`linear16` codec would need decoding before the fields can be promoted to Settings — decide whether to carry them forward or drop them*
- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-09.md) — Trinity-2's live camera, background and foreground controls are not reachable (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — simli-ai 2.1.1 exposes `setCamera(camera, lerp_seconds=...)`, `setBackground(id)` and `setForeground(id)` on `SimliClient`, which send the `CAM_`/`BG_`/`FG_` websocket messages; the merged PR #6400 added only the session's starting framing (`SimliVideoService.Framing`), so nothing can move the camera or swap an asset once the session is up — *needs a call: whether a mid-session control becomes a frame, a method, or both — AGENTS.md prefers a frame, and these are the first Simli controls that act during a session*
- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-09.md) — `enable_logging` is accepted but never used — simli-ai 2.x has no logging toggle (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — the constructor and the deprecated `InputParams` both take `enable_logging`, but it reaches neither `SimliConfig` nor `SimliClient`; simli-ai 2.1.1's reference documents no logging option either, only the stdlib logger named `simli_client` — *needs a call: a call between deprecating the argument and honouring it by configuring the `simli_client` logger*
- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-09.md) — Simli session events are never registered, so a session that dies mid-call is silent (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — a failed `start()` and a failed `send()` raise with Simli's own reason, which the service already turns into an `ErrorFrame`; a session Simli ends or errors *mid-call* still only ends the two stream iterators, with no `registerEventCallback` registered, `stopReason` and `error` unread, and nothing pushed upstream — *needs a call: which events become Pipecat event handlers versus an internal `push_error` on ERROR/STOP, and whether SPEAK/SILENT duplicate the audio frames the service already pushes*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The session is closed without `close_stream`, so pending audio is dropped and `is_last` never arrives (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `_disconnect_websocket` calls `websocket.close()` directly. The response-format page documents `{"type":"close_stream"}` followed by a message carrying `is_last: true` as the way to end a session, warning "Do not close the WebSocket connection immediately after sending", and the endpointing page describes Pipecat as sending `close_stream` when the call ends — which the service does not do. The `_process_response` docstring says `is_last` is "Only ``True`` after ``close_stream`` is sent at call end", so the branch it guards is unreachable. Three probe runs of `pulse` now (2026-10-12, 2026-10-28, this run) returned a final truncated at the same mid-sentence point while `pulse-2` returned the whole utterance, consistent with the tail being lost between the VAD-triggered `finalize` and teardown. — *needs a call: decide whether `stop()` should send `close_stream` and await the `is_last` final under a timeout before closing, or whether the docstring should simply be corrected to say the service never sends it*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `finalize_on_words` and `max_words` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Pulse's documented agentic pattern is `finalize_on_words=false` plus a client `finalize` per turn, which is what the service already sends on VADUserStoppedSpeakingFrame; the AsyncAPI bindings still default `finalize_on_words` to true, and the pulse-2 card tells clients not to send either parameter at all. — *needs a call: these change when finals arrive and pulse-2 must not receive them, so exposing them needs a stance on per-model gating and on whether the service should adopt the agentic pattern by default*
- [smallest/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/tts/2026-11-09.md) — complete_backoff_ms is not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — documented request field (0-10000 ms, default 4000) that holds the `complete` status back after the last audio chunk; the service stops its metrics on that status. Its sibling max_buffer_delay_ms is already a constructor argument. max_buffer_flush_ms stays out of scope, since the schema says it cannot be combined with context_id, which the service always sends. — *needs a call: whether this follows max_buffer_delay_ms as a constructor argument or becomes a Settings field, and whether shortening it is safe given it gates stop_all_metrics()*
- [soniox/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Soniox's real-time `translation` config object is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-25, 10 weeks) — v6 streams translation tokens on the same socket; the config takes `{type: one_way, target_language}` or `{type: two_way, language_a, language_b}` — *needs a call: translated tokens arrive interleaved with originals and marked `translation_status`, so exposing this needs a decision on how translations reach the pipeline (separate frames, or filtered out of the transcript)*
- [soniox/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/tts/2026-11-09.md) — `audio_format` accepts formats the service cannot configure or play back (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — Soniox's format reference gives sample rates and bitrates per format, while the service keeps one flat `VALID_SAMPLE_RATES` set for every `pcm_` format and sends `sample_rate` only for `pcm_` ones; the 2026-10-28 probe had `audio_format="pcm_mulaw"` at 24000 Hz pass the local check unwarned and Soniox answer 400 `invalid_request` ("allowed [8000]"), and mp3/opus/aac get neither `sample_rate` nor `bitrate`. Every non-PCM16 format also arrives as undecoded bytes in `TTSAudioRawFrame`. Format table and service code both unchanged this run. — *needs a call: whether to model Soniox's per-format rate/bitrate table and expose `bitrate`, or to narrow `audio_format` to the formats the receive path can actually play*
- [speechify/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechify/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Word timestamps collapse into a single word for languages written without spaces, which simba-3.5 made reachable (since 2026-10-28, 1 week) — `_SpeechMarkAccumulator` releases a word only when the next character in the text is whitespace or the text ends, so in Japanese, Chinese, Thai, Burmese and the other scriptio-continua languages simba-3.5 speaks, every mark abuts the previous one and merges into one. Re-probed live this run: a 14-word English sentence yields 14 word frames, a two-sentence Japanese one yields 1, audio fine in both. What degrades is `add_word_timestamps`, so bot-speaking text sync and word-level interruption tracking get one mark at the first word's time. Pipecat already has the shape of a fix twice over - `cartesia/tts.py` and `elevenlabs/tts_base.py` both detect Chinese/Japanese from the language setting and pass `includes_inter_frame_spaces=True` - but neither covers Thai or Burmese, and this service's problem is the merge rule rather than per-character tokens. — *needs a call: whether to follow the Cartesia/ElevenLabs CJK precedent and what it should cover, given simba-3.5 also speaks Thai, Burmese and Lao*
- [speechify/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechify/tts/2026-11-09.md) — The language map covers 6 of the 48 languages simba-3.5 speaks (since 2026-09-14, 8 weeks) — `language_to_speechify_language` maps DE, EN, ES, FR, IT, PT - the six simba-3.0 routes. simba-3.5 lists 48, so every other `Language` falls through `resolve_language` to its BCP-47 string with a `not verified` warning; re-checked this run for ja, zh, es-MX, en-GB, th and ko. Synthesis is unaffected (ja and es both probed fine). The Pipecat docs page still repeats the six-language list. — *needs a call: which tag each of the 48 gets - an identity mapping is right for es-MX and for the bare codes simba-3.5 lists, but en-GB or pt-PT would be a locale substitution that needs a stance*
- [speechify/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechify/tts/2026-11-09.md) — Speechify's WebSocket streaming-text endpoint `/v1/audio/stream/ws` has no Pipecat class; text is re-POSTed per aggregated utterance over HTTP (since 2026-10-12, 4 weeks) — Added 2026-10-08. Takes LLM text as it arrives and speaks each completed sentence, with `speech_marks=true` giving word marks on one session-wide timeline rather than the per-request timelines the service offsets itself. Settings are query-string parameters with the HTTP endpoint's names, so the payload logic carries over, `pronunciations` included (sent as a `session.pronunciations` message). Session limits shape the design - one concurrent request, closed after 60 s idle and after 5 minutes total. `GET /v1/audio/models` still does not list the endpoint under any model's `endpoints`. — *needs a call: whether to add a websocket sibling class at all, and what reconnect behavior the 5-minute session cap implies for a long call*
- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Default base URL is the undocumented `eu2` host although all four documented hosts serve `/agent` (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — The service defaults to `wss://eu2.rt.speechmatics.com/v2/agent`, still matching the SDK's own `DEFAULT_RT_URL`. Unchanged in the code this run; an earlier run established that `global`, `eu`, `us` and `au` all transcribe on `/agent`, so US and AU users are routed to the EU by default unless they set `base_url`. — *needs a call: whether to follow the SDK's default or switch to `global`, which routes to the nearest region but has data-residency implications*
- [speechmatics/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/tts/2026-11-09.md) — A sample rate other than 16 kHz makes every request 422 with an opaque error (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — the API's `output_format` is validated against `^(wav_16000|pcm_16000)$` (preview host's OpenAPI spec), but `_get_endpoint_url` builds `output_format=pcm_{sample_rate}`; with `sample_rate=None` (adopt the pipeline rate, commonly 24 kHz) this run's probe again got `Service unavailable [422]` and no audio, after only a warning at construction — *needs a call: whether the service should pin 16 kHz regardless of the caller's sample rate or raise at construction — pinning also has to force the rate on the frames it yields, so neither is a one-line change*
- [tavus/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/tavus/video/2026-11-09.md) — Tavus renamed persona/replica to PAL/Face; the class still takes `replica_id` only (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — The create-conversation reference documents only `face_id`/`pal_id` — `replica_id` and `persona_id` appear nowhere in its request schema — and Tavus's Pipecat integration page shows `TavusVideoService(api_key=..., face_id=..., session=session)` with `TAVUS_FACE_ID`, a snippet our constructor rejects with TypeError. Nothing is gated server-side: POST /v2/conversations with `replica_id` + `persona_id` returned 200 today, the list-faces page still records `/v2/replicas` and `replica_id`/`replica_ids` as supported aliases, and still gives no removal date. — *needs a call: a `face_id`/`pal_id` alias is a new constructor parameter plus a registry env-var change — needs a call on whether to follow the rename and how to deprecate `replica_id`*
- [tavus/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/tavus/video/2026-11-09.md) — Conversation `properties` are not reachable — `max_call_duration`, `apply_greenscreen`, recording, timeouts (since 2026-08-31, 10 weeks) — `TavusApi.create_conversation` posts a fixed body of `replica_id` and `persona_id`, so every Pipecat Tavus session takes the API defaults, including the 3600 s `max_call_duration` cap and the 300 s `participant_absent_timeout`. The fixed body also blocks the top-level fields, notably `policy: "eu"` for EU-resident conversations (set only at creation time) and `audio_only`, which Tavus's 10-15 changelog singles out as a now-faster path. The API accepts `properties` alongside the legacy fields, so a pass-through would work. — *needs a call: which knobs to expose, and where — the conversation is created once in `setup()` from constructor args, so a pass-through means new constructor parameters threaded through TavusVideoService, TavusTransportClient and TavusApi*
- [together/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/stt/2026-11-09.md) — `Settings.language` is never sent to the transcription endpoint (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_connect_websocket` still builds the URL from `model`, `turn_detection` and `input_audio_format` only. docs.pipecat.ai states the field is not sent and has no effect, so the harm is a capability gap (non-English users cannot force a language) rather than a misleading promise. The 2026-09-01 raw-socket probe showed the endpoint honours `&language=` — *needs a call: the stored default is `Language.EN`, so always sending it flips today's autodetect to forced English — send the stored value, or default the field to `None` and send only when set?*
- [together/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Interim transcriptions can never fire — the delta handler reads `transcript` but the event carries `delta` (since 2026-09-28, 6 weeks) — `_handle_transcription_delta` does `evt.get("transcript", "")`; Together's streaming page still documents the delta event's text field as `delta` (`transcript` belongs to the completed event), so the `InterimTranscriptionFrame` push is unreachable, as this run's probe again showed — no interim frame on either model. It is doubly unreachable because the class hard-codes `turn_detection=none`, under which the endpoint emits only a single `completed` event. docs.pipecat.ai still claims the service supports interim results — *needs a call: is the delta path meant to be reachable at all? `turn_detection=none` is deliberate (the class commits on `VADUserStoppedSpeakingFrame`), so fixing the key alone changes nothing — this needs a stance on whether interim results are a supported feature or the handler is dead code to drop*
- [together/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Together's streaming ASR models are usable but undocumented and untried as a default (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` and `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b` work through the class with no code change (no allowlist); parakeet again beat Whisper on this run's single sample (TTFB 191 ms vs 675 ms) with an equivalent transcript — *needs a call: Together's STT page still names no recommended model and no successor to Whisper; a default bump would need a stated successor plus repeat-median latency*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Tool results are always sent with Ultravox's defaults; `agentReaction` and `responseType` are not expressible (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `client_tool_result` accepts `agentReaction` (speaks | listens | speaks-once) and `responseType` (default tool-response; hang-up, new-stage), plus `errorType`/`errorMessage` and `updateCallState`; `_send_tool_result` sends only `invocationId` and `result`, so a handler cannot make the agent listen after a tool, end the call, or switch call stage — *needs a call: a call on how a function handler carries these — a result-string convention, a new frame, or a service-level default*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `user_text_message.urgency` is not user-expressible; injected text is never `immediate` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `_send_user_text` takes an `urgency` argument, but only the async-tool progress path passes it (soon | later); text from `InputTextRawFrame` and async-tool final results omit it and take Ultravox's documented default of `soon`, so nothing can interrupt the agent (`immediate`) and an application has no control at all; `threadId` (default `UI`) is omitted everywhere — *needs a call: urgency is per-message, so it needs a stance — a Settings default for all injected text, or a per-frame field*
- [whisper/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/whisper/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The default distil model ignores prompt conditioning, and both fields together drop speech (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — on `Systran/faster-distil-whisper-medium.en`, `hotwords` and `initial_prompt` each leave the transcript at "Pippecut", and setting both makes faster-whisper return only the first of the clip's two segments (reproduced again on this run's commit; earlier runs showed the surviving segment's `no_speech_prob` is 0.058, so our threshold is not what drops it). On `small.en` all four combinations keep both sentences and either field corrects "Pipecat". — *needs a call: whether to move the default off a distil checkpoint, suppress one field when the other is set, or just document that the pair is unsafe on distil models*
- [whisper/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/whisper/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Transcription knobs beyond hotwords and initial_prompt are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — `beam_size`, `temperature` (WhisperSTTServiceMLX has it, WhisperSTTService does not), `vad_filter`, `condition_on_previous_text` and `word_timestamps` are 5 of `transcribe()`'s 35 parameters that neither class passes, and `WhisperSTTServiceMLX.Settings` has neither `hotwords` (mlx-whisper has no such parameter) nor `initial_prompt` (it does); `BatchedInferencePipeline` (faster-whisper 1.1, ~4x faster) is unused. — *needs a call: which of these belong in Settings versus init-only inference config, whether batched inference pays off for single-utterance segments, and who can probe the MLX class, which needs Apple Silicon*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `audio.output.transport` accepts "binary" but the service only reads the JSON wire path (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the spec still says output "emits on the configured transport only", so setting it leaves `parse_server_event` with raw codec bytes it cannot parse and the conversation silent; input is safe either way because the server dual-accepts both channels, so only the output half is broken — *needs a call: a call on whether to implement binary framing on the receive side or drop `transport` from `AudioOutput` so the field cannot be set to a value the service does not honor*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — SIP calls are unreachable because the WebSocket URL is always built with `model` and never `call_id` (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — the spec lists `call_id` as a query parameter that binds the socket to an inbound SIP call and makes `model` ignored; the service already handles `input_audio_buffer.dtmf_event_received`, which xAI emits on SIP sessions only, so that path cannot be exercised today — *needs a call: a constructor or settings shape for `call_id` (it is per-call, not per-service) — more than a pass-through field*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — Session resumption cannot work because the service never reconnects with `?conversation_id=` (since 2026-09-28, 6 weeks) — `SessionResumption.enabled` is settable and `_conversation_id` is captured from `conversation.created`, but it is never read and `_connect` builds `?model=<model>` only, so opting in caches turns server-side that nothing ever replays — *needs a call: a call on whether the service should reconnect with its own cached `conversation_id` automatically or expose it so the application decides when to resume*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-09.md) — `web_search` and `x_search` tool options are unreachable, and passing them as a raw dict drops them silently (since 2026-09-28, 6 weeks) — xAI documents `location`, `allowed_domains`, `excluded_domains` and `enable_image_understanding` on `web_search`, and `excluded_x_handles`, `from_date`, `to_date`, `enable_image_understanding` and `enable_video_understanding` on `x_search`; `WebSearchTool` carries only `type` and `XSearchTool` only `allowed_x_handles`, and because the `GrokTool` union matches the typed model before `dict[str, Any]`, a raw dict is coerced to the model and the extra keys vanish without a warning — *needs a call: the domain and handle lists are documented as mutually exclusive pairs with maxItems caps, so exposing them needs a stance on validating that client-side versus letting the server reject*
- [xai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — xAI's streaming `format` parameter (inverse text normalization) is not reachable from Settings — `format=true` is documented on the WebSocket endpoint (requires `language`; available for ar, de, en, es, fr, ja, pt, ru, sv, vi, zh) and the live socket accepts it. `_build_ws_url` never sends it. The `language_to_xai_stt_language` docstring still claims setting a language applies ITN, which is now the separate `format` parameter's job. — *needs a call: a probe.py run cannot pass against this class while pre-ready audio is dropped, so the field cannot be verified the way a PR requires; land the drop fix first*
- [xai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — The service never sends xAI's `finalize` client message, so a final transcript waits for the silence timer (since 2026-09-01, 9 weeks) — xAI accepts `{"type": "finalize"}` (confirmed on the live socket) to close the current utterance immediately; Pipecat only sends `audio.done` on disconnect, so end-of-turn finals cost up to `endpointing` ms (server default 400ms) — *needs a call: a call on what triggers it — wiring it to the user turn-stop path is a behavior change that interacts with the turn strategies and with server-side `smart_turn`*
- [xai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/tts/2026-11-09.md) — XAITTSService cannot send xAI's WebSocket `replace` map via `session.update` — the TTS capability page now documents `{"type": "session.update", "replace": {...}}` on `wss://api.x.ai/v1/tts`, acknowledged with `session.updated`, applying to every utterance for the life of the connection. A probe sent it and the server echoed the map and synthesized normally. The change is a `replace` field on `XAIWebsocketTTSSettings` plus a `session.update` send in `_connect_websocket` (the existing reconnect-on-settings-change path re-sends it) and a `session.updated` branch in `_receive_messages`. Open PR 5562's description states the WebSocket endpoint has no equivalent, which is no longer true. — *needs a call: fold this into open PR 5562, which adds the same field to the HTTP class and would otherwise conflict, or land it as its own change?*
- [zoom_ai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/zoom_ai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Service sends `language` at the top level of `session.update`; Zoom's Live schema now documents it as `config.language` — every example and the field table on live-mode, text-polishing and speech-configuration put `language` inside `config`; `_session_update()` sets `update["language"]` and puts only model/language_detection/diarization/vocabulary in `config`. If Scribe ignores the top-level field, every session silently runs Scribe's default en-US. — *needs a call: a live check with a key — set a non-English language and read the effective `language` back from `session.updated` — before moving the field; no ZOOM_AI_API_KEY in this environment*
- [zoom_ai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/zoom_ai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Text Polishing combined with language detection is rejected by Scribe, and the service lets both be set — Zoom: Text Polishing "can't be combined with `config.language_detection`"; in Live it returns a nonfatal `invalid_config` error and the session update is not applied, so the whole session runs on defaults. `ZoomAISTTSettings` accepts `text_polishing=True` with `language_detection="continuous"`. — *needs a call: a call on whether to reject the combination client-side (validation in `__init__`/`_update_settings`) or only document it*
- [zoom_ai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/zoom_ai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Persistent Vocabulary Assets (`config.vocabulary_id`) have no Settings field — `vocabulary` only sends an inline phrase list; a reusable asset created at POST /aiservices/assets/vocabularies is reachable only through `extra` — *needs a call: Zoom says to use `vocabulary` or `vocabulary_id`, not both — exposing it needs a stance on the conflict; and no key here to probe the field*
- [zoom_ai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/zoom_ai/stt/2026-11-09.md) — Service depends on Scribe Live fields the published Live schema does not document — `turn_detection.silence_duration_ms`, `turn_detection: {"type": "none"}` and the `input_audio_buffer.commit` client event carry both turn-detection modes, but live-mode documents neither `turn_detection` nor any client event besides `session.update`, `input_audio_buffer.append` and `session.close`; those names are documented for the separate Voice Agent Live API. — *needs a call: confirmation from Zoom (or a live session reading back `session.updated`) that Scribe Live honors them, otherwise `silence_duration_ms` and `vad_force_turn_endpoint=True` are no-ops*
